===== PAGE 3 =====
Environmental Inspection & Control Services, LLC
Nye Country School District
Annual Notification of Asbestos
Management plan Availability
January 04, 2022
To: All Nye County School District Staff, Teachers, and Parents
For Immediate Posting

Dear Parents, Teachers, and Staff:

As required by Federal Regulations under the Asbestos Hazard Emergency Response Act
(AHERA), Nye County has recently completed the annual update of our asbestos management plans
for asbestos containing materials (ACM) in Nye County Schools. A number of our older Nye County
school buildings were built during the time when asbestos materials were in widespread use.
However, newer Nye County schools built after 1990 are essentially free of asbestos at this time.

The results of the recent periodic surveillance inspections for schools containing asbestos,
and other changes in the management plans over the past year are available for inspection at the
school administrative offices. Parents, Teachers and staff are encouraged to review this document

~ and use it for reference as necessary. School staff members are advised to maintain these documents
at the office of each Nye Country school, and make the management plan available to anyone
interested in reviewing the document. However, detailed questions regarding the asbestos
inspections, management plans, or other asbestos or environmental related questions should be
directed to Mr. Cameron Mc Rae, Nye Country School District Asbestos Program Manager (APM).
The Nye County schools that may be considered not to have any asbestos containing
materials at this time (with some exceptions such as roofing material) are as follows:
Tonopah High School Beatty High School
Pahrump High School Mt. Charleston Elementary School
J.G. Johnson Elementary School Silver Rim Elementary School
Round Mountain Elementary School Round Mountain Jr. / Sr. High School
Hafen Elementary School Transportation Office (1900 S. Woodchips)
Please call me at your convenience should you have any questions.
Regards,
Mr. Laurence G. Thir Mr. Joseph Schmidt
Asbestos Coordinator/Competent Person Maintenance & Operations Supervisor

===== PAGE 5 =====
Introduction

The information contained in this asbestos management plan is designed to assist
the Nye County School District to satisfy the requirements of recent regulations requiring
the inspection of school builds for the presence of asbestos containing building materials
(ACBM). These regulations include:

¢ The Occupational Safety and Health Administration (OSHA) Asbestos Standard

for the Construction Industry, 29 CFR 1926,1101.

¢ The Environmental Protection Agency (EPA) Asbestos Hazard Emergency

Response Act (AHERA), 40 CFR, Part 763.

¢ The EPA National Emission Standard for Hazardous Air Pollutants (NESHAP)

Standard for Asbestos 40 CFR, Part 61.

The recommendations made to the Nye County School District in the response
actions to the conditions found represent the least burdensome options available, while
still protection the environment and the health of building occupants and the public.
These recommendations also acknowledge the limited resources available in both time
and money to reduce the risks posed by asbestos in buildings. In choosing response
actions, it is necessary to prioritize the work to be done through the ranking of hazards
within each building, and the Nye County School District facilities as a whole.
Environmental Inspection & Control Services, LLC has provided this information to the
Nye County School District to aid in this decision making process.

Asbestos abatement is a costly, dangerous and disruptive activity. In general, it is
advisable to manage asbestos in place, as long as it does not pose a serious or immediate
hazard to building occupants and staff. However, in cases where an unreasonable risk is
present, or where the presence of asbestos limits the operation and function of a building
it should be modified or removed to eliminate these problems.


===== PAGE 6 =====
How to Use the Management Plan

This asbestos management plan was developed to assist building owners,
managers, administrators, employees and the public with the potential problems caused
by the presence of asbestos in buildings. It has been designed to comply with the
applicable rules for the inspection and reporting of asbestos hazards as provided in EPA’s
AHERA regulation for the management of asbestos in school buildings and OSHA’s ;
regulations concerning the control of asbestos during construction activities.

The asbestos management plan is divided into a number of sections to assist users
with finding needed information regarding the buildings covered under the plan. These
general components of the plan are as follows.

1.0 General Information

Introduction:

This section describes the overall objectives of the asbestos building inspection

program.

How to Use the Management Plan:

This section describes each of the components of the management plan, to give

the reader a brief description of various management plan sections.

Inspection Statement:

A summary of the methods used to conduct the inspection.

Building Information:

Summary information of various buildings inspected, and a description of the

information found in the Building Form report.
2.0 Building Inspection Information

How to Read Inspection and Response Action Forms

An overview of the information contained in the inspection and response action

report forms with information on their interpretation.


===== PAGE 7 =====
Building Forms

General building information, the list of asbestos suspect materials, and the
functional spaces where found.
Functional Space Inspection Forms
Listings of all suspect asbestos containing building materials found during the
inspection, and their condition.
Response Action Forms
The Management Pianner’s recommendations regarding the actions necessary to
reduce the hazards of asbestos in buildings,
Laboratory Results
Detailed information regarding the presence of asbestos in the bulk samples

collected during the inspection.
3.0 Operations and Maintenance (O&M) Plan

The O&M plan is the detailed description of the requirements and work practices
necessary to work with asbestos safely. It contains specific work practices for

: maintaining asbestos containing building materials in good condition, and for
removing small quantities of asbestos when necessary to complete other
maintenance activities.

4.0 Statement of Accreditation

This section contains copies of inspector and management planner certificates, as
well as the statement of qualifications from the asbestos analysis laboratories.


===== PAGE 8 =====
Inspection Statement

In December 2009, Environmental Inspection & Control Services, LLC (EICS)
obtained building information from the Nye County School District. The available
information included building lists and locations, floor plans, drawing, and results from
previous asbestos inspections.

Prior to inspection, contact was established though building maintenance to
schedule inspections and gain access to buildings. A representative of the Nye County
School District was made available to assist with building access. These building
maintenance personnel accompanied to inspectors, and were available to give relevant
building information, historical date, and occupancy information.

As required by the AHERA regulation, buildings were subdivided into Functional
Spaces and Sampling Areas from which bulk samples were obtained.

¢ A functional space is a room, area, or other building space that is unique as to

construction, function, and types of building materials present. Functional spaces

can be rooms, hallways, basements or other building areas that have a distinct
purpose. Most buildings will have many functional spaces, however small
buildings may sometimes be treated as one functional space. In this inspection
each distinct building area inspected has been assigned a functional space number,
such as 01, 02, etc to identify that space or area within the inspection and
response action reports.

e¢ Homogeneous sample areas are specific types of building materials that are

suspected of containing asbestos. To be assigned as a sample area, a building

component must appear to be made of the same material and have been installed
at relatively the same time in the buildings construction. Building materials that
have the same function, but appear different must be assigned different sample
area designations. Sample areas are unique within any given building, and are
assigned sample area designations, such as AA, AB, AC, etc, so that they may be
identified in the inspection and response actions reports.

In general, functional spaces (such as a room) can have many different samples areas
(drywall, ceiling tiles, insulation) within them, and sample areas can be found in many
different building areas (functional spaces).

Assessment information regarding the condition of the ACBM in building was
collected by the Building Inspector (BI). This information was analyzed by Management
Planner (MP) along with additional information regarding building occupancy and the
potential for asbestos exposure, to produce a hazard ranking system for the
implementation of response actions.


===== PAGE 9 =====
. Information contained in this asbestos management plan is of great value in
determining the presence of asbestos within buildings. However, to be useful in the
Lo future, it requires continued updates as conditions change and as asbestos containing
materials are removed from buildings. ;

===== PAGE 12 =====
How to Read Inspection and Response Action Forms
Functional Space Inspection Forms

The Functional Space Inspection Forms included in the management plan
provide written descriptions of the building areas inspected as required by 40 CFR
763.93. General building information, such as building name and address are included at
the top of this form. The number and name of the functional space area also given for
reference.

Information on the physical characteristics of the building are listed under
Physical Assessment Information. In this area the building inspector identifies physical
and occupancy issues that may have a bearing on potential human exposure to asbestos.

In the inspection information section, the homogeneous Sample Area code is
given for each suspect material identified in the specific functional space. These letter
cods are unique for each distinct material in the building being inspected; however they
do change from building to building for a group of buildings. For example, Sample Area
AA in Building 100 may be a completely different material as Sample Area AA in
Building 500.

The Sample Area Description gives the common name for the building material

/ that is suspect. Usually, the material identified, such as plaster, or spray on acoustical
ceiling material is also described in terms of its appearance or age, such as old ceiling
tiles, or white 9 x 9 floor tiles. This description helps with later identification.

The Sample/Assume/Previous section identifies if the material in this sample are
was actually samples as part of the inspection, assumed to be asbestos based on
appearance, age, or other factors, or was previously identified as asbestos in another
survey.

AHERA requires that asbestos containing building material be classified into one
of the three following categories:

e Thermal System Insulation (TSD) ;
e Surfacing Materials (SM)

e Miscellaneous Materials (MM)

The T/S/M/X section contains this information. Since not all asbestos containing

materials are building materials, but yet may be important from a health perspective,

these are also identified when noted during a building inspection.

The inspection information section also contains information on the quantities of
suspect asbestos material noted during the inspection. These are given in terms of
square or linear feet, number of pieces, and the approximate thickness of the material,
for calculation of volume if necessary.


===== PAGE 13 =====
The Building Inspector wil! categorize each suspect material as friable or non-

friable, based on the ability of the material to be crumbled or pulverized by hand

j pressure. Also the general condition of the material is given as damaged (D) or
undamaged (U). Undamaged materials may also be noted as in good condition (G)
or fair condition (F). Damaged materials may also be referred to as in poor (P)
condition.

The extent of damage is give as distributed (D) when the damage is widely
dispersed over the entire surface of the building material being inspected. Localized
(L) damage which is confined to one or two small areas of the material, rather than
over the entire surface. The approximate amount of damage is also given in terms of
percent (%). Materials that are friable, or which are made friable as the result of
damage, are also assessed in terms of their ability to shed fibers into the air. This is
addressed as a yes or no under the shedding section.

If repair is feasible (Repair Feasible), this is usually indicated only when the
material is accessible, undamaged, or only has localized damage in small quantities.
When repair is not feasible it is usually because the damage is extensive, or the
material is inaccessible. However, even when repair is feasible, there may be an
economic consideration to abate rather than repair.

AHERA assessment categories (1-7 + X) are given in the inspection information
to provide descriptions of the type of suspect material found in the functional space,
the relative condition, and amount of damage present for the material. These
categories are as follows:

AHERA Physical Assessment Categories: /

1. Damaged or significantly damaged TSI ACBM

2. Damaged friable surfacing ACBM

3. Significantly damaged friable surfacing ACBM

4, Damaged or significantly damaged friable miscellaneous ACBM

5. ACBM with potential for damage

6. ACBM with potential for significant damage

7, Any remaining friable ACBM or friable suspected ACBM.

X. Not applicable, material is non-ACBM or non-friable surfacing or

miscellaneous material.

These descriptions also relate to the mandatory response actions required for
certain materials under AHERA, which will be covered under the Response Action
section.

Lab results are given for each material, with concentration found given as a % by
volume. According to EPA and OSHA, materials with an asbestos concentration less
than 1% are not considered ACBM. However, where the asbestos laboratory has
identified a material as having trace quantities of asbestos detected, EICS recommends


===== PAGE 14 =====
that the material be treated as ACBM until it is re-samples to confirm action
concentration.

EICS’s sampling procedures utilizes a three dimensional coordinate system for
identifying the locations where asbestos bulk samples were taken during the inspection.
On Page 2 of the Functional Space form, the locations of any bulk samples taken in the
functional space are located using this system. The homogeneous sample area is
identified, along with the unique sample number given to the bulk sample.

A type of material (TOM) code is provided to further classify the material by
category of building material. These TOM codes are listed on page 2 of each form, and
are also referenced on the laboratory chain-of-custody sheets. ;

The reference door wall location indicates the door used as a reference for the
cootdinate system measurements. It gives the door location by compass direction of the
wall used, and should be interpreted as if your back was placed against the door indicated
facing into the room or functional space. Distance Front/Back indicates the direction
from the doorway to the sampling location, Distance Left/Right indicates the distance
left or right to the sample location, as measured from the door centerline. Distance
Vertical indicates the height of the sample, from floor level. All coordinate locations are
approximate, and are to the nearest foot.

Response Action Forms
: Response Actions are measures that must be taken based on the Management
Planner’s overall assessment of the physical condition of the asbestos materials present,
the potential risks to the building occupants based on occupancy and other factors, and
the likelihood of exposure based on the friability, concentration, and type of asbestos
present.

Like the Functional Space forms, basic information identifying the material and
functional space being assessed is given in the top section. The functional space number
and description for each functional space where the material is found is provided. The
quantity of material and friability is assessed for each functional space.

Response actions are give a numerical code of 1 through 7 under AHERA which
indicates both relative hazard of the material and the priority it should be given for action
to mitigate the hazard. The following table provides the relative rankings:


===== PAGE 15 =====
HAZARD RANKING UNDER AHERA
a Hazard Rank ACBM Condition ACBM Disturbance Potential
t 7 Significantly Damaged Any
6 Damaged Potential for Significant Damage
5 Damaged Potential for Damage
4 Damaged Low
3 Good Potential for Significant Damage
2 Good Potential for Damage
1 Good Low
While the above table is only one of several factors used in determining the
appropriate response action, it is generally indicative of the relative hazard and priority to
be used for the response actions given. Each hazard assessment given must be justified
by a brief summary of the logic used by the Management Planner in making the
assessment.
The Management Planner then provides the recommended response actions and
preventative measures to minimize the asbestos hazard. This recommendation must be
: the least burdensome, while still protecting human health and the environment. Dates for
the implementation of these response actions are also given.

SECTION 1. INTRODUCTION .


===== PAGE 176 =====
1. INTRODUCTION .
The comprehensive Operations and Maintenance (O&M) Program implemented by Nye
County School District (NCSD) encompasses a step-by-step asbestos control program as well as \
a medical surveillance program and respiratory protection program. Specific objectives of the
Program are to:

(1) Minimize the release of asbestos fibers, and protect maintenance workers,
building occupants and the public from airbome asbestos exposure, and

(2) Establish administrative and work procedures for maintenance activities involving
asbestos designed to achieve the first objective.

This Program addresses all types of ACM found in Nye County School District buildings
surveyed for asbestos-containing materials (ACM). ACM to be managed under the O@M
Program can be classified in one of the following categories:

A. Surfacing Material: ACM sprayed or troweled onto surfaces, such as decorative
plaster on ceilings or acoustical ACM on the underside of concrete slabs or
decking, or fireproofing materials on structural members.

B. Thermal System Insulation (TS): ACM applied to a building's thermal systems,
to prevent heat loss or gain, such as insulation on pipes, boilers, tanks, and ducts.

C. Miscellaneous ACM: Asbestos-containing ceiling or floor tiles, textiles, asbestos-
cement panels, and roofing materials are examples, ;

j Specific work procedures for ACM in each of these categories are found in the following
sections of this document, Section 7 - Work Practices for Surfacing Material, Section 8 - Work
Practices for Thermal System Insulation, and Section 9 - Work Practices for Miscellaneous
Materials.

NCSD’s O&M Program includes specific directions on how to deal with emergency
situations involving the release of asbestos. As long as ACM is present in a building a fiber
release episode is possible. Procedures for responding to such an emergency are found in Section
11 - Fiber Release Episodes.

O&M work practices are also provided for Cleaning Procedures in Section 6. Such
procedures will be employed to clean an area where asbestos dust and debris have accumulated,

Only trained persons are permitted to carry out the procedures set forth in the School
Districts's O&M Program. The Nye County School District has provided and will continue to

provide training for maintenance and custodial workers whose activities will, or potentially will,
disturb ACM. Trained O&M workers are covered by the Nye County's Medical Surveillance
Program and Respiratory Protection Program. These Programs are found in Section 3 - Worker
Protection.

To further insure worker protection the Nye County is conducting personal air monitoring
to document O&M worker exposure to airborne asbestos. The Nye County's air monitoring
program is discussed in Section 13. Additionally, the Nye County School District has taken steps
to alert and protect outside contractors from unintentional exposure to asbestos. This notification
plan is presented in Section 14,

1


===== PAGE 177 =====
Procedures for disposing of asbestos waste generated by Nye County O&M activities are ,
described in Section 13 - Disposal of Asbestos Waste, /
The Nye County School District O&M Program was developed in compliance with all
federal, state and local regulations. An overview of pertinent regulations is provided in Section 2
- Applicable Regulations.
Sections 4 and 10 of this document define O&M activities and Section 5 addresses the
Nye County's administrative framework for executing the O&M Program.

===== PAGE 179 =====
2, APPLICABLE REGULATIONS
2.1 OSHA ASBESTOS EXPOSURE REGULATIONS

The OSHA rule governing exposure to asbestos (29CFR 1910.1001 and 1926.1101 as
published in the Federal Register: 59 FR 40964-41 165, revised August 10, 1994) are relevant to
custodial, maintenance and construction-related activities in buildings. ‘This is a brief summary
of the Rule; the full text, found in Appendix A, should be consulted for a complete explanation.

' Three Regulations

The OSHA Rule is divided into three sets of regulations, covering general industry,
maritime, and construction. Companies or building owners engaged in building maintenance,
repair, renovation (including remodeling and decoration), and demolition, or in asbestos control
(enclosure, encapsulation, or removal of asbestos-containing material [ACM]) are covered by the
construction industry regulations. General industries are all other industries, except maritime and
shipbuilding, where workers may be exposed to airborne asbestos.

Companies considered general industry are primarily asbestos product fabrication and
repair companies, but also include any business which owns or rents buildings with ACM. For
example, both office workers and service personnel (custodians and maintenance staff) are
covered by the general industry regulations, while the service workers are also covered by the
construction industry regulations when they perform maintenance, repair, renovation, or asbestos
abatement tasks. Outside contractors (e.g.; electricians, plumbers, carpenters, asbestos
contractors) are covered by the construction regulations. Activities in the Nye County O&M
Program are governed primarily by the construction rather than the general industry regulations.

The standards cover exposure to asbestos containing materials, where the concentration
of the asbestos is greater than one (1%) percent. The standard assumes that certain building
materials known or suspected to contain asbestos, must be presumed to contain asbestos, when

: they are found in buildings built before 1981,
Exposure Standards
The OSHA asbestos regulations establish exposure standards for airborne asbestos, as
measured by phase contrast microscopy (PCM). Asbestos fiber concentrations above these
| standards trigger various worker protection controls and related requirements.
° Permissible Exposure Limits (PELs)
| Time-Weighted Average (TWA) -- 0.1 f/cc
| Excursion Limit (EL) -- 1.0 flec
i The first PEL, referred to as the TWA, is expressed as an eight-hour time-weighted
/ average; the EL is a 30-minute average. Air samples are to be collected as specified and
analyzed by phase contrast microscopy. If the PEL's are exceeded, employee information,
| notification, training, and medical surveillance programs must be initiated and a full compliance
| rogram must be undertaken. This includes engineering and work practice controls and
| prog gineering and work p :
respiratory protection for exposed workers. All air monitoring results and all actions taken by the
| employer must be fully documented, and records saved for specified periods, usually 30 years or
more.
3


===== PAGE 180 =====
The TWA exposure was designed to provide protection to asbestos exposure over an
entire workday, as would occur in asbestos product manufacturing and abatement. The EL
provides added protection to workers whose jobs periodically expose them to brief periods of
high concentrations of asbestos, such as occasional maintenance operations.

The current OSHA standards also introduce the concept of classes of asbestos work, with
increasing levels of control in proportion to increasing asbestos risks associated with different
tasks. Class I asbestos work is the highest level of exposure risk, related to the removal of
Thermal System Information (TSI) and Surfacing materials containing asbestos, Class II work is
abatement of miscellaneous materials, such as asbestos concrete pipe, roofing materials, floor
tiles, and other non-TSI, non-surfacing materials.

Class IIT work involves maintenance and repair operations involving all types of asbestos
containing building materials, including TSI and surfacing materials, where the activity is likely
to disturb the material. Class IV is the lowest risk category of asbestos work, which covers
maintenance and custodial activities. This class covers employees whose work may disturb
asbestos containing materials (ACM) or presumed asbestos containing materials (PACM).

Air Monitoring Requirements

Under the current OSHA rule, employers must make an initial exposure assessment
before or at the initiation of any operation where potential exposure to asbestos exists. The
exposure assessment must be based on sampling data conducted at the time work is initiated, or
through observation, experience, and past data which indicates the levels of asbestos exposure
employees would receive during the operation.

The latter method may require that the employee make a negative exposure assessment,
based on objective data that the task could not release airborne fibers in excess of the PEL's, or
where previous data exists for similar tasks that would allow the employers to conclude that no
exposure above the PEL's exists. The negative exposure assessment may also be made on the
basis of current monitoring data is below the PEL's for the worst case operation being performed,
and then applied to less hazardous tasks.

For Class I asbestos work, OSHA also requires that exposures be presumed to be in
excess of the PEL's, and must take appropriate protective measures, until a negative exposure
assessment is made.

To establish whether the TWA or EL is likely to be exceeded, air measurements must be
made under al] conditions likely to be encountered. In other words, air monitoring must be
conducted for all tasks to be undertaken in the vicinity of all types of ACM in question. Air
monitoring must be conducted by a trained and qualified individual, such as a Certified Industrial
Hygienist (CTH), Industrial Hygienist (IH), Occupational Health and Safety Technician (OHST),
or State of Nevada Licensed Asbestos Monitor.

Work Practice and Worker Protection Requirements

For construction work where the TWA or EL is exceeded, the worksite must be
segregated by barriers, warning signs must be posted, and decontamination facilities constructed.
In addition, air filtration equipment must be used to reduce fiber concentrations, Where ACM is
drilled, sanded, cut or otherwise abraded, special tools equipped with high efficiency particulate
air (HEPA) filtered vacuum lines must be used. A trained supervisor called a "Competent
Person" must oversee all asbestos related work, and be present on all job sites where ACM may
be encountered,

4


===== PAGE 181 =====
Workers must wear respiratory equipment appropriate to protect against the level of
asbestos fibers in the air. The minimum requirements for any worksite exceeding the TWA or to;
EL is a half-face, negative pressure air-purifying respirator with HEPA filters. Other protective
devices such as disposable coveralls may also be needed.
Class III and Class IV activities are granted certain exceptions to these requirements.
Class III work involves such activities as, pipe repair, valve replacement, installing electrical
conduits, installing or removing drywall, or roofing, and other general building maintenance
activities. Class ITV work involves cleaning, dusting vacuuming, and other maintenance and
custodial work where asbestos may unintentionally be disturbed. (Further elaboration is provided
in Section 4 of this document.) For these projects, barriers, air filtration equipment,
decontaminated facilities, and a Competent Person are not usually needed. However, warning
signs must be posted and respiratory equipment and HEPA-filtered cutting/grinding/sanding
tools must still be used.
State OSHA
Twenty-four states and U.S. territories have their own OSHA-approved occupational
safety and health plans. The State of Nevada has adopted and administers its own OSHA plan.
In the case of asbestos-related issues the State of Nevada OSHA plan generally parallels the
federal standards. However, there are some significant differences in the requirements for
licensing of asbestos related professionals. These requirements are outlined in Attachment B.
2.2. NATIONAL EMISSION STANDARDS FOR HAZARDOUS AIR POLLUTANTS
(NESHAP)
EPA's rules concerning the application, removal and disposal of ACM were issued under
NESHAP. Also included in NESHAP are rules concerning manufacturing, spraying and i:
fabricating of ACM. The full text of the NESHAP regulation is found in Appendix C.
Notification Requirements
At least 10 days advance notice must be filed with EPA's locally designated enforcement
agency when a building is to be demolished or "as early as possible before renovation begins," if
more than 260 linear feet of asbestos pipe covering or 160 square feet of asbestos surfacing
material are removed during building renovation. These quantities apply to ACM which is or
could become friable during renovation or demolition.
At least 20 days advance notice must be filed before demolition if the amount of friable
asbestos materials in the facility being demolished is less than 260 linear feet or 160 square feet.
| If less than 260 linear feet or 160 square feet of friable ACM is being removed at a
| facility being renovated, no notice is required.
The following information is required on the notice:
e Name and address of the building owner or manager;
° Description and location of the building;
e Estimate of the approximate amount of friable ACM present in the facility;
| ° Scheduled starting and completion dates of ACM removal;
5


===== PAGE 182 =====
° Description of planned removal methods;

° Procedures to be used to comply with the requirements of this regulation; and

° Name, address, and location of the disposal site.

Removal Requirements (if more than 260 linear feet or 160 square feet of friable ACM are
present)

Removal is required prior to demolition and prior to renovation if the renovation would
"break up" or make the ACM "inaccessible." This is interpreted as "disturbing" the ACM by
some EPA Regional Offices. Friable asbestos must be removed using wet removal techniques
unless special approval from EPA has been obtained to allow dry removal. No visible emissions
of dust to the outside air are permitted during removal or renovation.

Disposal Requirements

No visible emissions to the outside air are allowed during collection, packaging,
transportation, or deposition of ACM waste. Wet ACM must be sealed in leak-tight containers
and containers must be labeled with OSHA and U.S. Department of Transportation labels.
2.3. ASBESTOS HAZARD EMERGENCY RESPONSE ACT (AHERA)

In October 1986 the Asbestos Hazard Emergency Response Act (AHERA) was signed
into law. Included in this Act are provisions directing the EPA to establish rules and regulations
addressing asbestos-containing building material (ACM) in schools. Specifically, EPA was
directed to address the issues of: (1) identifying, (2) evaluating, and (3) controlling ACM in
schools.

Under a six-month deadline set by Congress, EPA convened a panel of representatives
from various associations and interest groups which would be affected by the regulation. The
panel members negotiated on many issues, and the results were published during April of 1987 in
the form of a proposed rule. The final AHERA regulations (rules) became effective December
14, 1987. They are found in 40 CFR 763 Subpart E 763.80 - 763.99, and are part of the Toxic
Substances Control Act (TSCA). The full text of AHERA is found in Appendix D,
Applicability

e All public and private elementary and secondary schools, in the U.S. and its

territories; and

° American schools on military bases in foreign countries.

Requirements

) To designate a person to ensure that AHERA requirements are properly

implemented;

® To inspect and identify friable and non-friable ACM;

e To monitor and periodically reinspect;

° To develop and update management plans;

6


===== PAGE 183 =====
. ° To determine and implement response actions;
° To develop and implement operations and maintenance programs;
° To notify parents, building occupants and outside contractors of ACM's identified
in the building;

° To employ accredited persons to perform required activities; and

° To document and maintain records on all activities.

This O&M Program is part of the Nye County School District's Asbestos Management
Plan. It has been prepared in compliance with OSHA regulations and AHERA requirements.
The O&M Program addresses each of the OSHA and AHERA-mandated elements: worker
protection; cleaning; operation and maintenance activities; and fiber release episodes.

3. WORKER PROTECTION
3.1 WORKER PROTECTION POLICY
It is the policy of Nye County School District to ensure that employees and/or those under
contract to the Nye County have received appropriate training, respiratory protection, protective
clothing and medical surveillance for any level of asbestos exposure, even below the OSHA
Permissible Exposure Limit (TWA) of 0.1 f/cc and the OSHA Excursion Limit (1 f/ec 30-minute
average). This policy exceeds the minimum requirements of Federal and State laws. Exposure
to other airborne contaminants is also considered in the program.
As required under State of Nevada regulations
The Nye County School District O&M Program is designed to minimize exposure to
airborne asbestos. Providing respiratory protection to workers is a crucial aspect of exposure
minimization. The OSHA asbestos construction standard requires establishing medical
surveillance and respiratory protection programs for workers whose exposure to asbestos fibers
exceeds or is expected to exceed established standards. Medical examinations are used to screen
workers who, for medical reasons, should not wear a respirator, and to obtain baseline medical
| data. For workers who qualify for respirator use, periodic medical exams track changes in
respiratory and other physiological functions. Respiratory programs are designed to insure that
workers who need to use respirators for specific jobs are (1) given the proper respirator for the
| task, (2) instructed in fitting the respirator to their face, and (3) trained in respirator care.
| The medical surveillance program found under 3.1 and respiratory protection program
p , eon]
| found under 3.2 were developed as part of this O&M Plan, and follow specifications in the
OSHA Asbestos Exposure Rule. The OSHA specifications speak to the frequency and types of
medical tests (i.e., medical history, respiratory disease questionnaire, physical exam, pulmonary
function tests, and, if deemed necessary by the physician, chest x-rays), the minimum respiratory
protection required for different levels of anticipated asbestos exposure, and various aspects of
i respirator fit-testing and care. These programs directly affect employees of the Nye County
whose activities may potentially expose them to asbestos. Furthermore, the Nye County will
insure that any outside contractor it employs whose activities may, or will cause exposure to
asbestos has implemented comparable medical surveillance and respiratory protection programs.

Asbestos Worker Exposure Rules -- 29 CFR 1926.1101 (Construction Industry),

Dz. Scope of the Medical Examination
1, A medical and work history questionnaire will be completed by the

cardiovascular and gastrointestinal systems. The initial
questionnaire is more extensive than the subsequent, annual

- Forced Vital Capacity (FVC)
- Forced Expiratory Volume At One Second
Test (FEV)

CFR 1926.1101. These may include a chest roentgenogram
(posterior - anterior 14 x 17 inches). The X-rays will be
reviewed by a Certified B Reader.

c. Any additional tests deemed appropriate by the examining
physician in accordance with 29 CFR 1926.1101.

will have completed a training course in spirometry sponsored by
an appropriate academic or professional institution.

employment.

combinations of these minerals).

2. Any recommended limitations on the employee or upon the use of
personal protective equipment such as clothing or respirators.

3. A statement that the employee has been informed by the physician
of the results of any medical conditions resulting from asbestos
exposure that require further explanation or treatment.

4. A statement that the employee has been informed by the physician
of the link between exposure to asbestos, smoking and lung cancer:

3.33. RESPIRATORY PROTECTION PROGRAM (Including Protective Clothing)

SCOPE: It is the policy of the Nye County School District to ensure that employees whose
assigned duties potentially expose them to asbestos are provided appropriate
training, respiratory protection and protective clothing.

GENERAL: The Nye County School District has established procedures to minimize health
risks for affected employees through a comprehensive Respiratory Protection
Program. This program will be reviewed at least annually and altered, as deemed
necessary, to provide for the safety and health of personnel. This program adheres
to the OSHA asbestos worker exposure rules: 29 CFR 1926.1101 (Construction
Industry), 29 CFR 1910.1001 (General Industry), and 1910.134 (Respiratory
Protection Regulations). Also referenced is 30 CFR 30, Part 11 (MSHA and
NIOSH) and EPA Publication 560-OPTS-86-001.

PROGRAM OPERATION:

° Affected Personnel: Employees of the Nye County School District, Facilities
Management, potentially exposed to asbestos, are included in this Program.
Exposure to other airborne contaminates in the course of asbestos-related work is
also encompassed by the Program. Compliance with all elements of the program
is mandatory for continued employment with the Nye County School District.

. Medical Examination: Each affected employee must attain clearance for use of a
respirator and for work in asbestos-contaminated environments through a
comprehensive medical examination administered by a licensed physician. See
Section 3.1.

. General: Wherever possible, the Nye County School District will institute —
engineering controls and work practices to reduce and maintain employee
exposure at or below the OSHA Action Level and OSHA Excursion Limit.
Where necessary, exposure will be measured by air monitoring using OSHA
protocols. Otherwise, objective data from similar settings will be employed to

| estimate expected exposure levels.
Where engineering controls or work practices are not feasible or inadequate to
reduce exposure to or helow the Action Level or Excursion Limit, employees will
use respirators appropriate for the exposure level. Respirators are provided at no
cost to the employee.
| In summary, the Nye County School District will utilize three steps in control of
respiratory hazards:
| - Assess the hazard -- air-monitoring or the use of objective data,
| - Reduce or eliminate the hazard -- engineering controls and work practices,
| and
| - Provide appropriate respiratory protective equipment -- Nye County
School District's Respirator Protection Program.
In all cases, the Nye County School District will provide more than the OSHA-
defined minimum protection for measured or estimated levels of exposure.
15
|
|


===== PAGE 192 =====
° Respiratory Hazards
A, Oxygen Deficiency - To be considered when working in confined spaces
or areas where oxygen may be deficient (less than 19.7% oxygen content
of the air) such as steam tunnels, boilers, mechanical chases or other areas.
B. Toxic Contaminants - Those likely to be encountered by Nye County
School District personnel:
1. Particulates - asbestos fibers
2. Gaseous materials (e.g., carbon monoxide, organic vapors) -
infrequently present
3. Combinations of particulates and gaseous materials
° Selection of Respirators
A. Respirator selection will be based upon the type of hazard and the
concentration of hazard through the guidance of the Program
Administrator.
All respirators purchased and used by the Nye County School District will
be approved by the Mine Safety and Health Administration (MSHA)
and/or The National Institute for Occupational Safety and Health (NIOSH)
as designated through:
- An assigned identification number associated with each unit, and
- A label identifying the type of hazard the unit is designed to protect
against.
Additional label information identifies the unit's limitations and the
component parts approved for use with the basic unit. Only the specified
manufacturer's replacement parts and filters will be used with each brand
name and type of respirator. Each affected employee will be issued a
respirator for his/her exclusive use.
B. Categories of Respirators
1. Air-Purifying, Negative Pressure Respirators (not for oxygen-
deficient environments)
a. Mechanical filter type for particulate contamination -- high
efficiency particulate air (HEPA) filters for 99.97%
removal of fibers >0.3 micrometers in diameter
b. Chemical cartridge type for gaseous contaminants (solvent
vapors)
; 16


===== PAGE 193 =====
2. Powered Air-Purifying (positive pressure) Respirators (not for
oxygen deficient environments) i}
a. Mechanical filter type
b. Chemical cartridge type
3. Supplied Air Respirators
a. Airline
1) Demand
2) Pressure-demand (EPA/NIOSH recommended)
3) Continuous flow
b. Self-contained breathing apparatus (SCBA)
C. Types of masks
lL. Half-face
2. Full-face -- includes helmet and hood types for powered air-
purifying and supplied air respirators
Dz. Protection Factors (PF) - The level of protection afforded by different
kinds of respirators.
1, PF = concentration outside mask divided by concentration inside (4
mask ,
2. PF is affected by the fit of the mask, worker activities and presence
of facial hair.
E. Respirators will be selected to provide more than OSHA-required
protection, as indicated in Table 3.1.
° Training
A. Each affected employee will receive respirator training upon hiring.
Instruction will include:
1. The medical aspects of asbestos exposure
2. Proper selection of respirator based upon the type and degree of
hazard
3, Proper cleaning, disinfection, and storage of respirators
4. Routine inspection of respirators
5. Proper storage of respirators

; 3. Medical reports of all users (see Section 3.1)
4, Laboratory reports of worksite air-monitoring

B. Medical and training records will be maintained for 30 years after

° Respiratory Equipment Fit-Testing and Fit-Checking

A, Fit-Testing shall be performed in accordance with OSHA Regulation 29
CFR 1926,1101 Appendix C as part of the respirator selection process,
and, at a minimum, annually. The procedures are summarized below; the
full fit test protocol is found in Appendix D. Fit-Checking will be
performed before each use.

B. Employees will be given the opportunity of choosing a respirator from
several brands and models to determine the one with the most comfortable
fit.

C. Employees with glasses must ensure that the respirator fits properly over
the corrective lens. Contact lens will not be worn during respirator use.

D. Fit-Testing Procedures
1. Qualitative Test -- conducted by qualified personnel

a. Irritant smoke (stannic chloride or titanium tetrachloride),
isoamylacetate, or saccharin test

1) The test is for air-purifying respirators with high
efficiency filters and for supplied air respirators.

2) The test for air-purifying negative pressure
respirators (as specified in 3 which follows) is to be
administered only if the negative and positive
pressure checks have been successfully passed.

3) The Process
a) Respirator wearer enters a test enclosure (a

clear suspended plastic hood or sufficient
substitute).

b) The challenge substance (irritant smoke or
alternative) is sprayed or squeezed into a
hole in the enclosure.

c) The wearer mimics several worker activities
and is required to speak during the test (refer
to the protocol set forth in OSHA 1926.1101
Appendix C).

20


===== PAGE 197 =====
d) If wearer detects the substance inside the 7
respirator, it indicates a defective fit; the td
mask fails the test.

NOTE: [f irritant smoke is used, this test must be

administered with caution because the smoke is

highly irritating to the eyes, skin and mucus
membranes. The eyes must be kept tightly closed
when testing a half-face, air-purifying respirator.
2. Quantitative Test -- Conducted in an approved laboratory to
determine a respirator's protection factor. Since respiratory
protection will be provided in excess of OSHA requirements, no
quantitative testing will be needed.
E, Fit-Checking Procedures
1, Proper placement of mask straps (outside of protective hood,
around crown of head and behind neck) is checked by a second
person.

; 2. Qualitative fit checking (for negative pressure respirators)

a. Negative pressure check: Wearer closes off inlets of

| filters/cartridges by covering with palms. Wearer inhales
so that facepiece collapses slightly and holds his/her breath
for ten seconds. Mask passes if there is no inward leakage

| and facepiece remains slightly collapsed.

: b. Positive pressure check: Wearer closes off the exhalation
valve of the respirator and gently exhales in mask. Proper
fit is obtained if a slight positive pressure can be built up

inside the mask without evidence of outward air leakage
around the facepiece.

e Resnirator Use
A. Employees are prohibited from entering all contaminated/hazardous areas
without the appropriate protective equipment.

B. Employees will wear protective equipment at all times while exposed to

asbestos contamination. Employees may exit the work site as they deem

: necessary for rest and relaxation.

C. Employees, as necessary for proper respirator fit, will shave facial hair.
Beards and goatees are prohibited for employees wearing respirators with
a fitted face-mask. Beards and goatees may be permitted for employees
using helmet or hood-type respirators.

i D. Procedures for removing respirators following work around ACM are

| discussed in the sections on work practice.

21
|

}


===== PAGE 198 =====
° Maintenance of Respirators
A. Routine inspection -- Before and after each use, the following checks for
defects must be performed. Note: Air-purifying respirators will be used
for most if not all anticipated work.
1. Negative pressure air-purifying respirators (half-mask and full
face-piece)
a. Rubber face-piece should be checked for:
1) Excessive dirt
2) Cracks, tears, holes
3) Distortion from improper storage
4) Cracked, loose or scratched lenses (full face-piece)
5) Broken or missing mounting clips
b. Headstraps should be checked for:
1) Breaks or tears
2) Loss of elasticity
3) Broken or malfunctioning buckles or attachments
4) Excessively worn serrations of the head harness
which might allow the face-piece to slip
c. Inhalation and exhalation valves should be checked for:
1) Detergent residue, dust particles or dirt on valve
; seal
2) Cracks, tears, or distortion in the valve material or
valve seal
3) Missing or defective valve covers
d. Filter elements should be checked for:
1) Proper filter for the hazard. :
2) Approved designation (MSHA/NIOSH)
3) Missing or worn gaskets
4) Worn thread
5) Cracks or dents in filter housing
2. Powered air-purifying respirators should be inspected for:
a. Face-piece, headstraps, valve and breathing tube, as above
b. Hood or helmet, (if applicable), headgear, and suspension
Cc. Cracks or breaks in face shield (if applicable)
d. Battery adequately charged
22


===== PAGE 199 =====
3. Supplied Air Respirators
a. Face-piece, headstraps and valves should be checked as
above.
b. Breathing tube should be checked for:
1) "Cracks
2) Missing or loose hose clamps
3) Broken or missing connectors
c. Hood, helmet, suits should be checked for:
1) Proper headgear suspension
2) Cracks or breaks in faceshield
3) Rips or torn seams
d. Air Supply System
1) Airline respirators should be checked for:
a) Breaks or kinks in air supply hoses and end
fitting attachments
b) Tightness of connections
c) Proper setting of regulations and valves per
manufacturer's recommendations
d) Correct operation of air purifying elements
and equipment malfunction alarms fo]
2) Self-contained breathing apparatus (SCBA) --
Consult manufacturer's instructions and literature
B. Routine cleaning -- after each use, the following procedures must be
followed:
1. Clean after each use with wet wipes and mild detergent.
2. Rinse thoroughly.
3. Wipe with alcohol pads.
' 4. Replace in plastic bag (see below).
5. When filters impede breathing, remove and discard them as
asbestos waste.
° Respirator Repair
A Repair must be performed by a qualified individual, usually at the
manufacturer's accredited repair facility.
B. Parts replacement must be with the specific manufacturer's items (different
producer's part are not interchangeable).
23


===== PAGE 200 =====
‘ C. The Program Administrator is responsible for maintaining a record of all
; repairs, documenting type of respirator and specific repair.
° Storage
A. Each respirator will be placed in the manufacturer's plastic bag and be
stored in the assigned closet by each employee.
B. The storage area(s) will not be exposed to dust, sunlight, soot, extreme
cold, excessive moisture, or contaminating chemicals.
C. A sufficient supply of alcohol pads, wet wipes, cleaning detergent and
replacement filters and, canisters will be available in the storage closet(s).
° Protective Clothing
A. Nye County School District personnel will wear protective clothing if
contamination of clothing or body, due to contact with asbestos, is likely.
B. Protective clothing will be necessary when entering contaminated areas
including, but not limited to:

1. Craw] spaces and tunnels

2. Work above a suspended ceiling

3. Where ACM is significantly damaged

; C. Type of protective clothing

1. Disposable double stitched coveralls, foot and head coverings, all
attached (foot coverings may be replaced by rubber boots or shoes
for safety and protection).

2. Sealed goggles, when using a half-mask respirator and where eye
protection is necessary.

3. Hard hats, safety shoes, hearing protection per demands of
worksite.

D~, Removal and disposal of protective clothing -- the following procedures
will be used when removing protective clothing:

1. Clean debris from garments with a HEPA vacuum cleaner.

2. Removal all protective clothing (not respirators) by rolling or
peeling the garments inside out off the body to prevent spread of
contamination,

3. Place clothing in 6-mil plastic bags labeled as asbestos-containing
waste with approved OSHA warning label - "Danger. Contains
asbestos fibers. Avoid creating dust. cancer and lung disease
hazard."

24


===== PAGE 201 =====
4. Remove respirator and place it in the storage bag.
° Respirator Program Evaluation
A. The Program shall be reviewed annually.
B. Adjustments will be enacted based upon relevant air sampling data,
changes in Federal and State laws, and the effectiveness of the Program.
SUMMARY: The Respiratory Protection Program, is designed to ensure the health and safety of
all affected employees with respect to particular work site conditions and
circumstances. The Nye County School District will provide frequent
surveillance and training to ensure all protective equipment is within the OSHA
specifications and NIOSH recommendations, and is used according to
manufacturers' specifications.
Employees are encouraged to submit recommendations to increase the usefulness
and scope of this Program. All requests, opinions and comments should be
directed to the Program Administrator. Copies of this program have been
submitted to all affected employees.
PROGRAM ADMINISTRATION
° The Program Administrator is responsible for:
A. Determining the feasibility of engineering or work practices that could
eliminate the need for respirators. Pog
B. Guidance in respirator selection and the purchase of MSHA/NIOSH
approved equipment.
C. Employee training and issuance of respirators.
D. Storage, maintenance, repair, and inspection of respirators.
E, Work area surveillance.
F, Recordkeeping.
G. Reviewing and updating this Program at least annually..

===== PAGE 203 =====
4. DEFINITION OF OPERATIONS AND MAINTENANCE ACTIVITIES

For work to legally be conducted by trained personnel under the Nye County School
District O&M Program, it must fall within the AHERA/OSHA definition of SMALL-SCALE,
SHORT-DURATION MAINTENANCE ACTIVITIES. Small-scale, short-duration maintenance
activities are tasks such as, but not limited to:

1. Removal of asbestos-containing insulation on pipes.

2. Removal of small quantities of asbestos-containing insulation on beams or above

ceilings.

3. Replacement of an asbestos-containing gasket on a valve.

4, Installation or removal of a small section of drywall
(asbestos-containing drywall or drywall with asbestos-containing joint
compound).

5. Installation of electrical wiring; telecommunication cables; or heating, ventilating
and air conditioning (HVAC) equipment through or proximate to ACM.

Small-scale, short-duration maintenance activities can be further defined, for the purposes
of the O&M Program, as:

1, Removal of small quantities of ACM only if required in the performance of

another maintenance activity not intended as asbestos abatement.

2, Removal of asbestos-containing thermal system insulation not to exceed amounts Py
greater than those which can be contained in a single glove bag (approximately 40
inches wide by 64 inches long).

3. Minor repairs to damaged thermal system insulation which do not require
removal.

4. Repairs to a piece of asbestos-containing wallboard.

5 Repairs, invalving encapsulation, enclosure or removal, ta small amounts of
friable asbestos-containing material only if required in the performance of
emergency or routine maintenance activity and not intended solely as asbestos
abatement. Such work may not exceed amounts greater than those which can be
contained in a single prefabricated mini-enclosure. Such an enclosure shall
conform spatially and geometrically to the localized work area, in order to
perform its intended containment function.

Specific procedures for work affecting any of the above are provided in Sections 7, 8 and

9 of this document.

Should maintenance activities exceed these restrictions for ACM disturbance, the work is
to be considered asbestos abatement and must be designed by an AHERA-accredited project
designer and be performed by AHERA-accredited abatement supervisors and workers.

5, OPERATIONS AND MAINTENANCE PROGRAM ADMINISTRATION Co
5.1 ORGANIZATION
The Nye County School District O&M Program is designed to function through the
interaction of individuals on three levels. The uppermost level in the organizational scheme is
the Nye County School District's Asbestos Program Manager who has ultimate responsibility for
all asbestos related activities. This duty is vested with the Director of Facilities Management.
Directly assisting the Asbestos Program Manager is the O&M Program Coordinator. This
individual has operational authority for implementing the O&M Program for the Nye County
School District. At the second level are the Maintenance supervisors working for Facilities
Management. These individuals will notify the O&M Program Coordinator of work activities
within Nye County School District facilities which will, or have the potential to, disturb ACM.
The third level is the Asbestos Maintenance Team. Asbestos Maintenance Teams consist of
trained personnel in the Facilities Maintenance Department. Work affecting ACM will be
conducted by trained personnel only.

O&M Program Coordinator

Nye County School District O&M Program Coordinator
The responsibilities of the Nye County School District O&M Program Coordinator
| include: directing the O&M work of the Maintenance supervisors, evaluating the effectiveness
| of the Program, and insuring that all facility records and asbestos management plans are
periodically updated to reflect O&M activities.
| This individual will be assigned to Facilities Management and work directly under the
| Director (Asbestos Program Manager). The O&M Program Coordinator will administer the
O&M Program, recommend policy and administrative changes, as well as sanctions for non-
| compliance, and implement the notification program to outside vendors as discussed in Section
| 15. This individual will also document and maintain records of O&M activities. The O&M
Program Coordinator will conduct periodic surveillance for Nye County School District
buildings (see Figure 9). Because the Coordinator will conduct periodic onsite inspections

===== PAGE 206 =====
during and following work procedures at buildings throughout the Nye County School District,
this person will monitor compliance with the O&M Program for the Nye County School District.
Awareness training sessions and O&M Asbestos Maintenance Team training will be arranged as
needed by the Coordinator. The Coordinator will arrange for contract cleaning services for Nye
County School District Buildings where initial cleaning is necessary. In addition this person will
order and ensure the availability of O&M related equipment and supplies.

The O&M Program Coordinator will receive and review job requests for O&M work,
issue permits, periodically inspect onsite O&M work completed or in progress. All requests for
work go directly to this individual. Since this position entails both administrative and oversight
responsibilities the Coordinator must have asbestos training. Accreditation under AHERA as a
Building Inspector and Management Planner and a minimum of 16 hours of training, as
described in AHERA for Operations and Maintenance activities, are recommended. The ability
to read architectural plans and familiarity with Nye County School District buildings is
advantageous.

Maintenance Supervisor

Supervisors will submit requests for O&M work to the Nye County School District O&M
Program Coordinator:, to include work such as Paint, Plumbing, Carpentry, Electrical work,
HVAC and Refrigeration, and General Maintenance. Any time maintenance activities will, or
may, affect ACM, the Maintenance supervisor will complete a job request form and submit it to
the Program Coordinator for evaluation. If the request is approved the Supervisor will receive
written confirmation and immediately issue notification of pending work to the affected
department. He will then assign trained personnel to conduct the job. At the conclusion of the
O&M activity the Supervisor will review, sign, and forward to the O&M Program Coordinator
the completed O&M Worker Checklist. All custodial and maintenance employees of the Nye
County School District must be informed about ACM through participation in awareness training
sessions. The Maintenance supervisor will notify the Program Coordinator when new employees
require training.

Instances of employee violations of Nye County School District's O&M Program will be
written up by the Maintenance supervisor. The Supervisor will discuss the violation with any
offender under his supervision. Documentation will be placed in the employee's personnel file
and reported to the O&M Program Coordinator and Department Director. The Director will
initiate disciplinary action in accordance with Nye County School District policy as deemed
appropriate.

O&M Asbestos Maintenance Team

Trained Nye County School District personnel will be on call for all O&M related
activities. Asbestos Maintenance Team members will be notified by their Supervisor of pending
work and a schedule will be determined. The Asbestos Maintenance Team will conduct all work
in accordance with the procedures set forth in the Nye County School District O&M Program.
At the conclusion of all work the Asbestos Maintenance Team will complete the required paper
work. All Asbestos Maintenance Team personnel will have O&M training and participate in the
Nye County School District's Medical Surveillance and Respiratory Protection Programs.

5.2. ADMINISTRATION

A work permit system is the backbone of the Nye County School District's O&M
Program. It is designed to control activities so that disturbance of ACM can be avoided or
minimized.

30


===== PAGE 207 =====
All maintenance work involving ACM, or work near ACM will be evaluated on a case-
by-case basis by the O&M Program Coordinator. A Job Request and Permit Form (see Figure 1)
submitted by the Maintenance supervisor initiates this process. If approval to conduct the work
is granted, the Program Coordinator will issue a permit number and return the Approval Form to
the Maintenance supervisor. The Supervisor will notify the affected department (see Figure 8)
and then deploy trained personnel into the field. The original Job Request Form will be placed in
the permanent files maintained by the O&M Program Coordinator with copies going to the
Supervisor and the assigned Asbestos Maintenance Team.

Upon approval by the Coordinator it may be determined that the O&M activities will
require personal air monitoring. If air monitoring is indicated on the Work Permit, workers must
report to the Coordinator who will complete the Personal Air Monitoring Checklist (see Figure 5)
and issue workers a personal air monitor.

During or following O&M work procedures the O&M Program Coordinator may conduct
periodic work inspections (see Figures 6 & 7) to insure that all work is being conducted in
accordance with the Nye County School District's O&M Program.

At the conclusion of maintenance work, the O&M Worker Checklist (see Figure 2) will
be completed by the Asbestos Maintenance Team. The form will be returned to the Supervisor
for review. A copy will be kept by the Supervisor, The original will be returned to the O&M
Program Coordinator for review and inclusion in the Nye County Schoo! District's records.

In the event of a fiber release episode, onsite personnel will inform the O&M Program
Coordinator immediately. The Coordinator will determine whether it is a minor or major episode
and instruct onsite personnel on immediate actions to be taken at the site as per the Nye County
School District's O&M Program (see Section 11), The O&M Program Coordinator will then
arrange for Nye County School District trained personnel to respond to a minor fiber release pa
episode. ‘The Asbestos Maintenance Team will proceed to the site where the episode occurred,
conduct appropriate response actions, and will fill out a Fiber Release Episode Report (See
Figure 3), The form will be sent to the O&M Program Coordinator. In the event of a major fiber
release an outside contractor will be called upon to respond.

A determination of the need for initial cleaning of any Nye County School District
building with friable ACM will be made by the O&M Program Coordinator in consultation with
the Nye County School District Director of Facilities Management. Outside contractors will be
retained to specifically provide AHFRA initial cleaning services. Upon completion of the
cleaning, the Contractor will fill out the Record of Initial Cleaning Form (Figure 4) and send it to
the O&M Program Coordinator.

6. CLEANING REQUIREMENTS AND PROCEDURES

6.1 REQUIREMENTS AND PROCEDURES

In all buildings containing friable ACM, outside contractors under contract with the Nye
County School District will clean up the dust and debris using procedures described for initial
cleaning of asbestos materials in Section 6.

Although dust and debris cleaned up during routine cleaning (after the initial cleaning)
will not contain significant amounts of asbestos, custodians must be careful not to disturb ACM.
For this reason, all custodians will be instructed about the location of all accessible ACM. Any
dust or debris produced by disturbing ACM MUST NOT be cleaned up with routine cleaning
techniques. Such a disturbance must be considered a fiber-release episode. Procedures in
Section 11 will be followed when responding to a fiber-release episode.

42


===== PAGE 219 =====

existing asbestos dust and debris will be Respirator, Disposable Suit, Signs, Hot

area prior to cleaning. This includes the

construction of critical barriers over all

openings and the use of air filtration units with HEPA filters to maintain low levels of airborne

fibers. If the amount of debris is equal to, or less than three square feet the contractor will be

instructed to secure the area by locking doors or by setting up a temporary barrier and posting the
following warning sign: Caution. Construction Work Area, Do Not Enter.

1, The O&M Program Coordinator will contract for the services of an outside abatement
contractor to conduct the initial cleaning. Cleaning will be scheduled when the building
is not in use.

2. The air-handling system in the area where the work is to be performed will be shut off.

3. All cleaning personnel will wear appropriate respiratory protection and disposable
clothing.

4, Carpet, upholstered furniture, curtains, drapes, and other fabric hangings or surfaces will Oe
be cleaned with a hot water extraction cleaner.

5. Hangings, books, and other objects will be HEPA vacuumed.

6. All horizontal surfaces will be cleaned using a HEPA vacuum cleaner and/or wet cleaning
procedures as follows:

° Auy aiea wiih accumulated visible debiis will first be IEPA vacuumed.

° Wet disposable rags or toweling by immersing in a container of water, wring out
excess water.

° Fold rag or toweling.

° Wipe surface to be cleaned, re-fold to a fresh area of rag. Continue until all clean
surfaces of the rag have been used.

) Dispose of rag or toweling in an ACM disposal bag.

e Wet the mop head in a bucket of water; squeeze excess water out of mop.

e Mop small area; frequently re-immerse mop in water.

° Collapse bag, twist shut, bend top over, and seal with duct tape by wrapping

° Wash hands, neck, and face with soap and water.

7, WORK PRACTICES FOR SURFACING MATERIAL ;
71 GENERAL CONSIDERATIONS FOR SURFACING MATERIAL
Surfacing material is defined as material that is sprayed-on, troweled-on, or otherwise
applied to building surfaces. Included are fireproofing materials on structural members,
acoustical or decorative treatment plaster on ceilings or walls, drywall finishes and other
materials applied for similar purposes. The following rules govern work affecting surfacing
ACM:
° ALL MAJOR CONSTRUCTION WORK (remodeling, renovation, expansion)
affecting surfacing material WILL BE DELAYED UNTIL THE ACM IS
REMOVED. In such instances the Nye County School District will employ
AHERA-accredited project designers, abatement contractor/supervisors, and
abatement workers to plan and execute the removal.
e ALL MAINTENANCE OR INSTALLATION WORK affecting surfacing
material that is LARGER THAN A SMALL-SCALE, SHORT-DURATION
PROJECT will be treated as an ASBESTOS ABATEMENT PROJECT, as above.
Py ALL SMALL-SCALE, SHORT-DURATION MAINTENANCE WORK WILL

document for definition). Permission to conduct the work will be granted via a

’ For work which involves entering the space above suspended ceilings (cable installation,

some heating, ventilating and air conditioning - HVAC - maintenance, electrical or plumbing
repair), and which may disturb ACM or ACM dust or debris located there, the procedures
outlined below will be employed in the order indicated.

Two groups of procedures are described:

® Work which requires that the HVAC system be in operation ("on"), and

e Work which can be conducted without the HVAC operating.
7.2A HVAC System Balancing and Related Work

For work which requires that the HVAC system be in operation such as system balancing,
the following procedures will be followed. If an outside HVAC contractor will participate in the
project, all preparatory and cleanup tasks should be performed by the Nye County School District
Asbestos Maintenance Team members.

1. A Request and Approval Form will be submitted by the Maintenance supervisor
to the O&M Program Coordinator for approval. If the work request is approved
the Form will be returned to the Maintenance supervisor, and the Supervisor will
schedule a Asbestos Maintenance Team to conduct the work, If the work request
is denied, the Coordinator will return a copy of the Form to the Maintenance
supervisor with an explanation for the denial, and retain the original for the Nye
County School District permanent file.

; 2. Work will be scheduled when the building or floor is not in use. Otherwise,
persons will be evacuated from that part of the building where the work is tobe -
performed. An official notice will be sent to personnel in the area announcing the
scheduled work and requesting cooperation.

3. The assigned Asbestos Maintenance Team will obtain all the necessary equipment
for the task from the central supply (see box). A set of procedures will also be
issued to the Asbestos Maintenance Team to accompany them on the job.

Warning Signs, Respirator, Disposable

4. The work area will be secured by locking doors or by setting up a temporary
barrier. Do not block access to any emergency exits and post a warning sign:
Caution. Construction Work Area. Do Not Enter. ;

5. The air-handling system in the area where the work is to be performed will be shut
off for initial work activities and activated again just before balancing the HVAC
is begun.

6. Trained Asbestos Maintenance Team members and any outside contractors will
wear their assigned respirator. In addition, full-body disposable suits (including
hoods) are to be worn; booties are not recommended for climbing ladders.

7. The HEPA air filtration device will be positioned just below the opening to the
space above the ceiling, and will be run in the recirculation mode for the duration
of the project. Do not allow the discharge air stream to blow directly on ACM or
ACM dust and debris.

8. Existing dust or debris will be cleaned from the floor, furniture, equipment, and
other surfaces in the immediate location of the work. A HEPA vacuum and/or
wet cleaning techniques will be used for this task.

9. Furniture and equipment will then be moved from the immediate area where the
work is to be performed.

10. Items unable to be moved from the immediate work area will be draped with 6-
mil plastic (polyethylene) sheets.

11, If the floor is covered with carpet, a 6-mil plastic sheet will be placed underneath
the work site and will extend approximately six feet beyond.

12, All equipment and materials will be assembled, including that needed for the
repair, maintenance or installation activities.

13. | When entering the space above a suspended (dropped) ceiling system, the
following procedures will be observed:

@ Move ceiling tiles slowly and carefully to gain access to the space.

® Move the first tile far enough to permit a fine mist of amended water -- a
solution of 50 parts water and one part detergent -- to be sprayed through
the opening in the air space above.

6 When the first tile has been moved far enough to allow room for a vacuum
hose, clean the edge of the tile and the ceiling grid (track) with a HEPA
vacuum cleaner and gently move the tile over a neighboring tile.

' ® HEPA vacuum the top of the tile and any other tiles that may be disturbed
during the course of the work.
® HEPA vacuum visible debris on ceiling splines, HVAC mixing boxes,
| light fixtures or elsewhere in the vicinity of the work.
e Mist the general area above the ceiling with amended water.

If the ceiling is fixed (hard) rather than suspended, entry will be made through a
; trap door or access panel. Misting and vacuuming should be conducted as
; described for suspended ceilings. Be sure to vacuum parts of the ceiling over
which crawling or walking will be necessary.

14. Care must be exercised to minimize contact with the in-place ACM and ACM
dust and debris during the work.

15, Dust and debris on HVAC mixing boxes and related components will be cleaned
with a HEPA vacuum cleaner before maintenance work is performed.

16. Mixing boxes will be opened; inspection of the boxes and cleaning of screens
inside the boxes will be conducted with minimal vibration to system components.

17. The HVAC system will be activated and the open end of the intake tube of the
HEPA air filtration unit positioned above the ceiling near the work to be
performed. Workers will proceed to balance the HVAC system, make air flow
measurements, and conduct related work which requires the HVAC system to be
operating.

18. Small amounts of ACM which must be contacted to conduct the repair or install
new equipment should first be removed. This includes the installation of new
utility hangers, (Existing utility hangers will be used for short runs of new, pipe,
conduit, etc. where possible — suspended ceiling hangers should not be used for
this purpose). Limited ACM removal will be conducted as follows:

e Attach an intake tube to the HEPA air filtration unit and place the open
end of the tube close to the area of ACM removal.

® Lightly but repeatedly mist with amended water the area of and
immediately surrounding the ACM to be removed and any other ACM
which is likely to be disturbed.

®@ Remove the ACM with a putty knife and catch removed material in a
hand-held can or paint tray. If ACM falls to the ceiling or floor, clean it up
immediately with a HEPA vacuum cleaner.

19. Any ACM dislodged in the process of conducting work will be cleaned up with a
HEPA vacuum cleaner.

20. After completing the project, the work area will be cleaned with a HEPA vacuum
and the tile carefully replaced.

® Vacuum the bottom side of each tile moved for access.

e Vacuum the floor if a plastic sheet was not used.

® Wipe all tools and equipment including ladders with damp cloths and pass
them to a worker off the plastic sheet (if used). The cloths/toweling are to
be left on the sheet.

® If plastic sheeting was used to drape unmovable objects, mist the plastic
with amended water and carefully remove folding the exposed surface to
the inside. Place in an ACM disposal bag.

===== PAGE 227 =====
® Workers will mist each other down and then remove their suits turning
them inside out while doing so. Persons on the sheet step off as each leg
of the suit is removed leaving their suit on the plastic. Dispose of the suits {
in an ACM disposal bag if a plastic sheet is not used.

21. The plastic sheet will be misted with amended water and folded over its contents
toward the center. The folded sheet will be placed in an ACM disposal bag.

22. Workers will thoroughly clean their respirators and face/head with wet wipes.
Place wipes in ACM disposal bag.

23. The disposal bag will be twisted shut, and the twisted neck bent over and sealed
with duct tape by wrapping the tape around bag neck several times.

24. Workers will now remove their respirators.

25. Labeled disposal bags will be removed from the site by the Asbestos Maintenance
Team and transported to the temporary storage site. Eventually the accumulated
bagged waste materials will be transported to an approved disposal site in
conformance with applicable regulations. At the time of disposal, the Asbestos
Maintenance Team will provide the Nye County School District with
documentation of disposal including, but not limited to, waste manifests.

26. An O&M Worker Checklist will be completed by the Asbestos Maintenance
Team. This form is to be reviewed and signed by the Maintenance supervisor
who will retain a copy for his files. The original will be sent to the O&M
Program Coordinator to be placed in the Nye County School District's permanent
file.

7.3 EXPOSED SURFACING MATERIAL
All maintenance work, repairs, or installation of new equipment which involves

disturbance of asbestos-containing walls, ceilings, drywall with joint compound, and acoustical
or decorative applications will be performed as specified below.

Three groups of procedures are described:

. Minor repair/patching of the ACM,

e Direct manipulation (drilling, sawing, etc.) of the material for installation of

equipment, and
° Incidental disturbance of material.
7.34 Repairing Surfacing Material

Minor repairs to damaged ACM should be undertaken as soon as is practical to minimize
additional damage and fiber release. Minor is defined as less than one square foot. Repairs to
friable ACM are more urgent than nonfriable ACM.

Incidental Disturbance to Surfacing Material
Activities such as changing light bulbs will occasionally necessitate incidental contact
with asbestos-containing surfacing material. Such activities can be performed by regular
Maintenance supervisor janitorial/ maintenance staff once the immediate surrounding area has
been encapsulated if the material is cementitous or otherwise able to accept an encapsulant. The
O&M Program Coordinator or outside qualified abatement contractor should make this
determination.

8.1A Repair

In cases of minor tears or gouges in the jackets or insulation material on pipe runs or

other thermal insulation, repair can be performed.

1. A Request and Approval Form Warning Signs, Respirator,

2. Work will be scheduled when the building is not in use. Otherwise, persons will
be evacuated from that part of the building where the work is to be performed. An
official notice will be sent to personnel in the area announcing the scheduled work
and requesting cooperation.

3. The assigned Asbestos Maintenance Team will obtain all the necessary equipment
for the task from the central supply (see box). A set of procedures will also be
issued iv ihe Asbesius Maiuieuaiice Team to accompany them on the job.

4, The work area will be secured by locking doors or by setting up a temporary
barrier. Do not block access to any emergency exits and post a warning sign:
Caution. Construction Work Area. Do Not Enter.

5. The air-handling system in the area where the work is to be performed will be shut
off.

6. Trained Asbestos Maintenance Team members will wear their assigned respirator
and full-body disposable suits (including hoods for overhead work).

7. Existing dust or debris will be cleaned from the floor, furniture, equipment, and
other surfaces in the immediate location of the work. A HEPA vacuum and/or
wet cleaning techniques will be used for this task.
