===== PAGE 240 =====
. 8. Furniture and equipment will then be moved from the immediate area where the
; __ work is to be performed.

9. Items unable to be moved from the immediate work area will be draped in 6-mil
plastic (polyethylene) sheets.

10. If the floor is covered with carpet, a 6-mil plastic sheet will be placed underneath
the work site and will extend approximately six feet beyond. If the floor has an
irregular surface, such as wood or tile, a 6-mil plastic sheet will be placed
underneath the work site and will extend approximately six feet beyond.

11. Ifa hole penetrates the outside wrap and into the insulation, it should be sprayed
with amended water -- a solution of 50 parts water and one part detergent -- and
then filled with a non-asbestos product, (fiberglass, patching mud, plaster of paris,
etc.).

12. Once the hole is filled, the tear in the cover will be repaired. Plaster bandaging
material is recommended for this purpose. Rubber gloves should be used with the
plaster bandaging material.

13. Plaster bandaging material is wrapped around the pipe or elbow to cover the tear,
using care to bond the material to the pipe. Allow it to dry completely before
contact.

14. After completing the repair, tools and equipment including ladders are to be
wiped with wet cloths/toweling and moved off the plastic sheet (if used). The
cloths/toweling are to be left on the sheet or placed in an ACM disposal bag.

15. If plastic sheeting was used to drape unmovable objects, mist the plastic with
amended water and carefully remove folding the exposed surface to the inside.
Place in an ACM disposal bag.

16. HEPA vacuum the floor.

17. Workers will mist each other down and then remove their suits turning them
inside-out while doing so. Place suits directly in an ACM disposal bag if a plastic
drop cloth on the floor was not used. Persons on the plastic sheet will step off
leaving their suit on the plastic.

18. Mist the plastic drop cloth with amended water and fold the plastic and its
contents toward the center and place in an ACM disposal bag.

19. Workers will thoroughly clean their respirators and face/head with wet wipes.
Place wipes in disposal bag.

20. Collapse bag, twist shut, bend top over, and seal with duct tape by wrapping
around bag neck several times,

21. Workers will now remove their respirators.

22. Labeled disposal bags will be removed from the site by the Asbestos Maintenance
Team and transported to the temporary storage site. Eventually the accumulated

, 8B Removal
When it is necessary to remove small amounts of asbestos insulation to perform repair
work on the thermal system network, a glovebag is required to remove the ACM. No more ACM
can be removed than will fit into a single glovebag.
NOTE: This procedure cannot be performed on hot pipe over 125 degrees Fahrenheit; the bag
will melt.

1, A Request and Approval Form :
will be submitted by the Warning Signs, Respirator, Disposable
Maintenance supervisor to the Clothing, HEPA Vacuum, Disposable
O&M Program Coordinator Cloths/Toweling, Mop, 6-mil Plastic
for approval. Ifthe work Sheets, Duct Tape, Glovebag, 2 Utility
request is approved the Form Knives, Spray Bottle, Detergent, Survival
will be returned to the Saw (Optional), Sponge or Rag, Nylon
Maintenance supervisor, and Bristle Brush, Spray Encapsulant/Plaster
the Supervisor will schedule a Bandage Material, ACM Disposal Bags,
Asbestos Maintenance Team Bucket with Water, Wet Wipes, Request
to conduct the work. If the and Checklist Forms
work request is denied, the
Coordinator will return a copy :
of the Form to the 8.1B Equipment Needed For Glovebag

: . . Removal .
Maintenance supervisor with
an explanation for the denial,
and retain the original for the Nye County School District permanent file.

2. Work will be scheduled when the building is not in use. Otherwise, persons will
be evacuated from that part of the building where the work is to be performed. An
official notice will be sent to personnel in the area announcing the scheduled work
and requesting cooperation.

3, The assigned Asbestos Maintenance Team will obtain all the necessary equipment
for the task from the central supply (see box). A set of procedures will also be

wet cleaning techniques will be used for this task.

66


===== PAGE 243 =====
9. Items unable to be moved from the immediate work area will be draped in 6-mil
plastic (polyethylene) sheets. : f 4

10. If the floor is covered with carpet, a 6-mil plastic sheet will be placed underneath
the work site and will extend approximately six feet beyond. If the floor has an
irregular surface, such as wood or tile, a 6-mil plastic sheet will be placed
underneath the work site and will extend approximately six feet beyond.

il. Preparation of the pipe insulation is as follows: Duct tape will be wrapped around
the pipe insulation approximately three feet apart so that the glovebag can be
attached and removed without tearing the cover on the insulation. This will
remain in place once the glovebag has been removed.

12. The glovebag should be put around the pipe by slitting the bag up the side seams.
Leave enough room so that when the bag is sealed along the sides and top, access
to the insulation has not been restricted.

13. The seams which were slit are sealed with duct tape from the top of the pipe to the
top of the glovebag.

14. Any tools which will be needed to remove the insulation from the pipe must be
added to the glovebag. (See list of suggested tools in Box 8.1B.)

15, The glovebag is then sealed with tape along the top, sealing the front and back of
the bag together. The pipe should now run through the bag above the level of the
gloves.

16. Next, the gap in the glovebag around the pipe is securely sealed with duct tape.
This tape should be attached to the duct tape that was originally wrapped on the
pipe to protect the jacket.

17. At this point, the glovebag should have an airtight seal around the pipe insulation.
Tools should be in the bag and gloves should be accessible to the worker and the
pipe insulation.

i8. To remove a section of insulation, fusi mist duwai ihe entive surface of the pipe
within the glovebag with amended water -- a solution of 50 parts water and one
part detergent.

19. Using a knife or survival or bone saw, cut the canvas and insulation around the
circumference of the pipe at the ends of the section to be removed. Ifa survival
saw is being used, be cautious not to cut through the pipe.

20. Make a horizontal cut with a utility knife to connect the two cuts made in the
previous step. During the cutting process the spray bottle should be used to
continue wetting the material.

21, The insulation can now be gently pulled off the pipe and eased, NOT DROPPED,
to the bottom of the bag. Continue to mist the material as each section is placed
in the bottom of the glovebag.

io}
67


===== PAGE 244 =====
. 22. The pipe should next be cleaned; a sponge may be sufficient to remove any

) residual material left by the insulation, or a nylon bristle brush might be needed to

get all the insulating material from the scale and rust left on the pipe.

23, Once the pipe is cleaned and all insulating material removed, the exposed ends of
the insulation need to be sealed. This can be done with a spray encapsulant (many
brands are commercially available), or the ends can be sealed using the procedure
for pipe insulation repair, (see Section 8.1A of this O&M Program).

24, The tools need to be removed from the bag before the bag is removed from the
pipe. The simplest way is to grasp the tools and pull the gloves inside-out. The
workers hands are now free, and the reversed gloves containing the tools should
be twisted closed and taped in two places about 2 inch apart. Using a utility
knife, cut through each glove between the two pieces of the tape. The twisted and
taped glove, now cut free from the bag, is put in a bucket of water.

25. When the tools have been removed the glovebag can be taken off the pipe. To do
this, first evacuate the air from the bag using a HEPA vacuum and the vacuum
port side of the bag. When the air is removed, twist the bag in the center and tape
the twisted portion with two pieces of duct tape to seal the waste in the lower half
of the bag.

26. Cut the twisted portion between the two strips of tape so that the bottom is
separated from the top of the bag and remains sealed. The top is still around the
pipe but is sealed where the bottom of the bag was removed.

: 27. Cut or remove the tape from around the pipe where it goes through the bag. Then
cut along the bag horizontally to remove the remaining top portion of the bag
from the pipe. Fold the bag in on itself as it is removed so that the inside of the
bag, which is contaminated, is not exposed.

28. All waste should be placed in a labeled ACM disposal bag.

29. After completing the repair, tools and equipment including ladders are to be
wiped with wet cloths/toweling and moved off the plastic sheet (if used). The
cloths/toweling are to be left on the sheet or placed in an ACM disposal bag.
Tools inside the glove will be cut free while submerged. They are cleaned in the
bucket then removed. The glove will be placed in a disposal bag. Water may be
flushed down the drain; thoroughly rinse bucket.

30. If plastic sheeting was used to drape unmovable objects, mist the plastic with
amended water and carefully remove, folding the exposed surface to the inside.
Place the sheeting in an ACM disposal bag.

31. HEPA vacuum the floor.

32, Workers wil! mist each other down and then remove their suits turning them
inside-out while doing so. Place suits directly in an ACM disposal bag if a plastic
drop cloth on the floor was not used. Persons on the plastic drop cloth will step
off leaving their suit on the plastic.

68


===== PAGE 245 =====
33. Mist the plastic drop cloth with amended water and fold the plastic and its
contents toward the center and place in an ACM disposal bag. fo}

34. Workers will thoroughly clean their respirators and face/head with wet wipes.
Place wipes in the ACM disposal bag.

35. Collapse the disposal bag, twist shut, bend top over, and seal with duct tape by
wrapping around bag neck several times,

36. Workers will now remove their respirators.

37. Labeled disposal bags will be removed from the site by the Asbestos Maintenance
Team and transported to the temporary storage site. Eventually the accumulated
bagged waste materials will be transported to an approved disposal site in
conformance with applicable regulations. At the time of disposal, the Asbestos
Maintenance Team will provide the Nye County School District with
documentation of disposal including, but not limited to, waste manifests.

38. An O&M Worker Checklist will be completed by the Asbestos Maintenance ,
Team. This form is to be reviewed and signed by the Maintenance supervisor
who will retain a copy for his files. The original will be sent to the O&M
Program Coordinator to be placed in the Nye County School District's permanent
file.

39. Proceed with maintenance and/or repair activity.

, 82 DUCT TAPE AND SEAM TAPE
Asbestos duct tape and pipe insulation seam tape can be handled in a couple of ways
depending upon the type of material with which they are associated. The two products are
similar and can be treated in the same manner. Repair is basically a process of covering the
damaged material to prevent further damage or fiber release. Repair should not be attempted in
cases where tape is delaminating from the surface or hanging loose.
8.2A Repair

The following procedures must be used when minor repair is needed on duct tape found

on HVAC joints.

1. A Request and Approval Form

will be submitted by the Warning Signs, Respirator, Disposable

Maintenance supervisor to the Clothing, HEPA Vacuum, Mop,

O&M Program Coordinator Disposable Cloths/Toweling, 6-mil

for approval. If the work Polyethylene Sheeting, Spray Adhesive,

request is approved the Form Vinyl Duct Tape, Utility Knife, ACM

will be returned to the Disposal Bags, Spray Bottle, Detergent,

Maintenance supervisor, and Wet Wipes, Request and Checklist Forms

the Supervisor will schedule a

Asbestos Maintenance Team 8.2A Equipment for Tape Repair

to conduct the work. If the

work request is denied, the

Coordinator will return a copy of the Form to the Maintenance supervisor with an
) explanation for the denial, and retain the original for the Nye County School

District permanent file.

be evacuated from that part of the building where the work is to be performed. An

8.2B Removal
Removal of more than two joints covered with asbestos duct tape should be considered
beyond the scope of an O&M activity and not be performed by trained O&M workers, For
removal of more than two joints, the project must be conducted as an asbestos abatement project
and the Nye County School District requires the use of an AHERA accredited project designer,
contractor, and abatement workers.

will be submitted by the Clothing, HEPA Vacuum, Disposable

for tin snips to cut away a section of pipe insulation (as opposed to metal duct).
NOTE: This procedure can only be done when the insulating material on the pipe
does not contain asbestos. If the insulating material is ACM, see procedures in
Section 8.1.

. 83 CEMENT ASBESTOS PIPE/FLUE

Minor maintenance work and repairs which involve cement asbestos pipe/flue will be
performed as specified below. Under the Nye County School District O&M Program intentional
disturbance to cement asbestos pipe/flue is not permitted. Should modification to these asbestos-
containing materials be required the Nye County School District will have them removed by
accredited personnel.
Repairing Cement Asbestos Products

Minor repairs to damaged cement asbestos pipe/flue may be undertaken under this O&M

Program. However, extensively damaged pipe/flue will be removed. Removal will be conducted
as an asbestos abatement project and requires the use of an accredited designer, contractor, and
abatement workers.

8.4A Repair

In case of minor cracks, tears or gouges in jackets or insulating material on boilers, tanks,
flues, and similar thermal system components, repairs can be performed.

1. A Request and Approval Form Warning Signs, Respirator, Disposable
will be submitted by the Clothing, HEPA Vacuum, Disposable
Maintenance supervisor to the Cloths/Toweling, Mop, 6-mil Polyethylene
O&M Program Coordinator Sheeting, Spray Bottle, Detergent,
for approval. If the work Lightweight Patch Material, Plaster
request is approved the Form Bandaging Material, Spray Adhesive,
will be returned to the Reinforcement Cloth, Rubber Gloves,
Maintenance supervisor, and ACM Disposal Bags, Wet Wipes, Duct
the Supervisor will schedule a Tape, Request and Checklist Forms
Asbestos Maintenance Team
to conduct the work. If the 8.4A Equipment for Boiler and Tank Insulation
work request is denied, the Repair.

Coordinator will return a copy

of the Form to the

Maintenance supervisor with an explanation for the denial, and retain the original
for the Nye County School District permanent file.

2. Work will be scheduled when the building is not in use. Otherwise, persons will
be evacuated from that part of the building where the work is to be performed. An
official notice will be sent to personnel in the area announcing the scheduled work
and requesting cooperation.

3. The assigned Asbestos Maintenance Team will obtain all the necessary equipment
for the task from the central supply (see box). A set of procedures will also be
issued to the Asbestos Maintenance Team to accompany them on the job.

4. The work area will be secured by locking doors or by setting up a temporary
barrier. Do not biock access to any emergency exits and post a warning sign:
Caution. Construction Work Area. Do Not Enter.

5. The air-handling system in the area where the work is to be performed will be shut
off.

6. Trained Asbestos Maintenance Team members will wear their assigned respirator.
In addition, full-body disposable suits (including hoods) are to be worn.

7. Furniture and equipment will be cleaned of any debris using a HEPA vacuum
and/or wet cloths/toweling. The furniture and equipment can then be moved from
the immediate area where work is to be performed.

8. The area to be repaired and the floor immediately below and extending 10 feet in
all directions will be HEPA vacuumed, wet mopped or wiped with wet
cloths/toweling.

79


===== PAGE 256 =====
. 9. Items unable to be moved from the immediate work area will be draped in 6-mil
plastic (polyethylene) sheets.

10. If the floor is covered with carpet, a 6-mil plastic sheet will be placed underneath
the work site and will extend approximately six feet beyond. If the floor has an
irregular surface, such as wood or tile, a 6-mil plastic sheet will be placed
underneath the work site and will extend approximately six feet beyond.

11. Using a spray bottle with amended water -- a solution of 50 parts water and one
part detergent -- lightly mist the area around the tear.

12. Ifa hole or cracks are present, these should be filled with a non-asbestos product
(fiberglass, plastic cement, high temperature caulk or other material which is
compatible with moderate expansion and contraction).

13, Once the hole or cracks are filled, the tear in the cover can be repaired. Plaster
bandaging material is recommended for horizontal surfaces; spray adhesive and
canvas is recommended for vertical surfaces or old, dirty surfaces.

14. For clean, horizontal surfaces, plaster bandaging material is placed over the tear
and smoothed by hand. Ensure that areas where plaster bandaging is to be applied
has been dampened with water and is free of dirt, dust or debris. Extend patch
approximately 2"-4" beyond damaged area, Allow to dry completely before
contact.

For vertical or dirty surfaces, HEPA vacuum at least six inches beyond damaged

, area to remove any loose dirt, dust or debris. Apply spray adhesive or contact
cement liberally at least six inches beyond damaged area. Apply same adhesive or
glue to one side of canvas patch. Extend new canvas jacket patch at least six
inches beyond damaged area using adhesive or glue to hold. Ensure that all loose
ends of the patch are glued down or trimmed flush.

15. After completing the repair, tools and equipment including ladders are to be
wiped with wet cloths/toweling and moved off the plastic sheet (if used). The
cloths/toweling are to be left on the sheet or placed in an ACM disposal bag.

16. If plastic sheeting was used to drape unmovable objects, mist the plastic with
amended water, and carefully remove folding the exposed surface to the inside.
Place in an ACM disposal bag.

17. Workers will mist each other down and then remove their suits turning them
inside out while doing so. Place suits directly in an ACM disposal bag if a plastic
drop cloth was not used. Persons on the plastic sheet will step off leaving their
suit on the plastic.

18. Mist the plastic sheet with amended water and fold the plastic and its contents
toward the center and place in an ACM disposal bag.

19. HEPA vacuum the floor, if a plastic sheet was not used.

20. Workers will thoroughly clean their respirators and face/head with wet wipes.
Place wipes in disposal bag.

80

around bag neck several times. ro)

8.4B Removal
When boiler or tank insulation is damaged beyond repair, removal may be necessary. All
work beyond the scope of an O&M activity must be conducted as an asbestos abatement project
and requires the use of an AHERA accredited project designer, contractor, and abatement
workers.
NOTE: This procedure may not be used if greater than three square feet of material must be
removed, or if the insulation and support wire is not securely attached to the boiler or tank.
1. A Request and Approval Form Warning Signs, Respirator, Disposable
will be submitted by the Clothing, HEPA Vacuum, Disposable
Maintenance supervisor to the Cloths/Toweling, Mop, 6-mil Polyethylene
O&M Program Coordinator Sheeting, Spray Bottle, Detergent,

request is approved the Form needed), Reinforcement Cloth, Rubber
will be returned to the Gloves, Wire Cutters, ACM Disposal Bag,

the Supervisor will schedule a Request and Checklist Forms

Coordinator will return a copy
of the Form to the

for the Nye County School District permanent file.

===== PAGE 259 =====
8. The area to be repaired and the floor immediately below and extending 10 feet in :

ll. Pick away insulation around the circumference of the area to be removed down to
the wire mesh by slowly and carefully prying small pieces of insulation loose and
into the HEPA Vacuum hose inlet.

12. Cut wire mesh support to enable section of damaged material to be removed in
one piece.

the disposal bag and possibly cause injury if not boxed.

14. Place boxed insulation in an ACM disposal bag or wrap in 6-mil polyethylene
sheets, tape and label.

15. Spray exposed edges of in-place insulation with a penetrating encapsulant.

16. Fill void in insulation with fiberglass or other suitable lightweight asbestos-free
material.

17. Proceed with jacket repair starting with item 11 under 8.4A.

NOTE: If damaged area is one square foot or larger, use fiberglass reinforcement tape and
lightweight plastic/fiberglass cement to patch damaged area. If too much weight is placed on the
damaged area, the material may be further damaged.

8.5 GASKET MATERIALS
Removal

Asbestos containing gaskets are used in applications such as boilers, tanks and valves.
Any type of system utilizing gaskets is covered by the procedures which follow. Examples of
components separated or connected using a gasket are: boiler clean-out cover plates, tank
inspection cover plates, and valve packing insulating rope on steel furnace doors.

Disturbance to any system components connected or sealed with an asbestos containing

gasket, must be undertaken with care to avoid excessive fiber release.

1. A Request and Approval Form Warning Signs, Respirators, Disposable
will be submitted by the Clothing, HEPA Vacuum, Mop,

Maintenance supervisor to the Disposable Cloths/Toweling, 6-mil
O&M Program Coordinator Polyethylene Sheet, Spray Bottle,

the Supervisor will schedule a

: 9. Items unable to be moved from the immediate work area will be draped in 6-mil

ll. Prior to separating the individual components, saturate the exposed portion of the
gasket with amended water --a solution of 50 parts water and one part detergent.

12. As the components are separated, continue to further saturate the gasket material
with amended water.

13. After the components are separated, saturate complete gasket area, remove
asbestos gasket material by scraping with a sharp chisel or utility knife. Care
must be taken to avoid damaging the sealing surface of the component.

14. If the gasket material is very difficult to scrape, try a commercially available
penetrating agent such as wall paper remover. This type of penetrating agent will
help soften the material.

15. DO NOT dry serape or use a wire brush to clean gasket material. These
techniques will result in significant fiber release.

16. After the gasket is removed, use wet rags or a sponge with a scouring pad to clean
any remaining asbestos residue.

17. All debris will be placed in an ACM disposal bag.

18. Gaskets will be replaced with asbestos-free products.

19, After completing the repair, tools and equipment including ladders are to be
wiped with wet cloths/toweling and moved off the plastic sheet (if used). The
cloths/toweling are to be left on the sheet or placed in an ACM disposal bag.

amended water, and carefully remove folding the exposed surface to the inside.

drop cloth was not used. Persons on the plastic sheet will step off leaving their
suit on the plastic.

; 9. WORK PRACTICES FOR MISCELLANEOUS MATERIALS

9.1 FLOOR TILE AND MASTIC
9.1A Maintenance

Floor tile identified as containing or assumed to contain asbestos MUST NOT be sanded
or otherwise abraded; self-polishing waxes should be used unless wax stripping, reapplication,
and polishing can be accomplished using wet techniques and without abrading the tile.
9.1B Repair

Repair and replacement of single isolated tiles or no more than ten side by side tiles can
be conducted under this O&M Program. Not all trained Asbestos Maintenance Team members
will be proficient at this task. In order to avoid chipping and breaking tile experienced persons
should be assigned to carry out the following procedures:

1. A Request and Approval Form Warning Signs, Respirator, HEPA
will be submitted by the Vacuum, Wall Scraper, Mallet, Hot-air
Maintenance supervisor to the Gun/Hand-held Hair Dryer, Non-toxic
O&M Program Coordinator Solvent, Spray Bottle, Detergent, ACM
for approval. Ifthe work Disposal Bags, 6-mil Polyethylene Sheets,
request is approved the Form Duct Tape, OSHA Labels, Disposable
will be returned to the Cloths/Toweling, Wet Wipes, Extension
Maintenance supervisor, and Cord, Request and Checklist Forms
the Supervisor will schedule a
Asbestos Maintenance Team = 9,1B Equipment for Floor Tile Repair
to conduct the work. If the
work request is denied, the
Coordinator will return a copy of the Form to the Maintenance supervisor with an
explanation for the denial, and retain the original for the Nye County School
District permanent file.

2. Work will be scheduled when the building is not in use. Otherwise, persons will
be evacuated from that part of the building where the work is to be performed. An
official notice will be sent to personnel in the area announcing the scheduled work
and requesting cooperation.

3. The assigned Asbestos Maintenance Team will obtain all the necessary equipment
for the task from the central supply (see box). A set of procedures will also be
issued to the Asbestos Maintenance Team to accompany them on the job.

4. The work area will be secured by locking doors or by setting up a temporary
barrier. Do not block access to any emergency exits and post a warning sign:
Caution. Construction Work Area. Do Not Enter.

5. Trained Asbestos Maintenance Team members will wear their assigned respirator
and full-body disposable suits.

===== PAGE 265 =====
6. The area to be repaired will be HEPA vacuumed.
\
7. Tiles to be removed will be cautiously pried loose using a wall scraper
(approximately 4" blade and 6" to 8" handle) and mallet.
r Tiles which receive least traffic should be selected for starting the
removal, (Ordinarily they come up easiest.)
° Carefully wedge the wall scraper in the seam of two adjoining tiles and
gradually force the edge of the tile up and away from the floor. DO NOT
break off pieces of the tile but continue to force it up by working the
scraper beneath the tile exerting both a forward pressure and a twisting
action on the blade to promote release of the entire tile.
° Where the adhesive is heavy, the scraper may be pushed by striking the
handle with a mallet. Use blows of moderate force, maintain the scraper at
a 25 to 30 degree angle to the floor,
IF TILES CHIP, BREAK, OR OTHERWISE DISINTEGRATE,
SUSPEND WORK. HEPA VACUUM THE DEBRIS.
Alternative Procedure J (for difficult-to-remove tiles):
° Thoroughly heat each tile to be removed with a hot air blower/hair dryer.
Heat penetrating the tile will soften the adhesive. Resume above
procedure on heated tile to pry loose.
IF ALTERNATIVE I IS INADEQUATE, SUSPEND WORK. HEPA VACUUM
| THE DEBRIS.
Alternative Procedure I (if Alternative I is ineffective):
° Apply a non-toxic solvent (a citric acid based product is preferred) around
the edges of each tile, and allow to soak according to the manufacturer's
directions. Resume original procedure to pry tiles loose.
: 8. A spray bottle containing amended water -- a solution of 50 parts water and | part
detergent -- will be used to mist the immediate area in the event a tile breaks.
Broken edges will likewise be sprayed and broken tiles will be placed in an ACM
disposal bag.
9, Tiles removed intact will be stacked on a 6-mil polyethylene sheet, wrapped and

taped with duct tape, no more than six to a bundle. Each bundle will be labeled
| with an OSHA warning label.
10. Adhesive remaining on the floor will be carefully scraped off.

° Mist adhesive with amended water and scrape it up with smooth, easy
strokes. Wipe adhesive off the scrapper onto a wet rag and place the rag

into an ACM disposal bag.

9.2 FLOOR SHEETING (LINOLEUM) AND MASTIC :
/
9.2A Maintenance
Sheet flooring identified as containing or assumed to contain asbestos MUST NOT be
sanded or otherwise abraded. Although the ACM is typically found in the backing to the
linoleum, some products may have asbestos in the top layer as well. No special attention is
needed for waxing operations since stripping prior to waxing is typically not performed.
9.2B Repair
Repair by replacement of small areas of sheet flooring (up to 3 square feet) will be
conducted as follows:

. 93 CEMENT ASBESTOS BOARD

All maintenance work, repairs, or installation of new equipment which involves

disturbance of cement asbestos board will be performed as specified below.

Two groups of procedures are described:

° Minor repair/patching of the ACM, and
e Direct manipulation (drilling, sawing, etc.) of the material for installation of
equipment.

9.4 FIRE DOORS

Fire doors WILL NOT be altered or modified in any way under the Nye County School
District O&M Program. All known and assumed asbestos-containing fire doors have been
posted with AHERA warning labels.

It is the policy of the Nye County School District to remove all known and assumed
asbestos-containing fire doors intact in conjunction with renovation or remodeling or should
modification to the door itself be required, e.g., cutting a window in the door.

Doors which are removed will be taken off their hinges, wrapped in a double layer of 6-
mil polyethylene and taped closed with duct tape. Door hardware, signs and any attachments will
not be recycled. They will remain in place and be disposed of as asbestos waste as directed by
the O&M Program Coordinator.

11. FIBER RELEASE EPISODES

11.1 FIBER RELEASE EPISODE RESPONSE

As long as ACM remains in Nye County School District buildings covered
by this O&M Program, a fiber release episode could occur, Trained Nye County School District
Asbestos Maintenance Teams will be called upon to respond to minor episodes; an asbestos
contractor with AHERA accredited supervisors and workers will be retained to clean up after
major episodes.
11.2 Minor Fiber Release

Minor episodes are defined in EPA guidelines (and AHERA requirements)
as less than three square feet or three linear feet of ACM fallen or having been dislodged.
Response actions should utilize standard wet cleaning and HEPA-vacuum techniques:
Immediate On-site Response

Warning Signs, Respirators, Disposable

1. Evacuate Clothing, Spray Bottle, Detergent, Mop,
persons from Disposable Cloths/Toweling, HEPA
that part of the Vacuum, ACM Disposal Bags, Penetrating
building where Encapsulant, Wet Wipes, Duct Tape, and
the release Fiber Release Report Form
occurred.

11.2 Equipment for Minor Fiber Release

2. Shut-off or
modify the air-
handling system
to the area to prevent contamination of other areas of the building
serviced by the same air-handling system.

3. Secure the area by either physical means or by scheduling,
Emergency exits must remain functional.

4. Post signs to prevent unauthorized entry to the area: .
Caution. Construction Work Area. Do Not Enter.

5. Contact the O&M Program Coordinator.

Follow-up Response

6. The O&M Program Coordinator will notify and authorize a
Asbestos Maintenance Team to proceed to the site of the minor
fiber release and proceed with clean-up activities.

7. Trained Asbestos Maintenance Team members will wear their
assigned respirator with HEPA filters and full-body disposable
suits (including hoods).

8. Thoroughly saturate debris with amended water -- a solution of 50
parts water and one part detergent, using a mister with a fine spray.

104


===== PAGE 279 =====
9. HEPA vacuum, wet mop or wipe up debris with wet disposable
cloths/toweling.

10. Place debris in labeled ACM disposal bags.

11. HEPA vacuum all surfaces, (i.e., floors, walls, furnishings, pipes . .
) in the area.

12. If practical, damaged ACM will be repaired with asbestos-free
material or sealed with an encapsulant.

13. Workers will mist each other down and then remove their suits
turning them inside out while doing so. Place the suits directly in
an ACM disposal bag.

14, Workers will thoroughly clean their respirators and face/head with
wet wipes. Wipes will be placed in an ACM disposal bag.

manifests.

Maintenance Team documenting the incident and the work. This
form is to be sent to the O&M Program Coordinator who will
review the report, complete the information and place it in the Nye
County School District's permanent file.

dislodging of more than three square or linear feet of friable ACM. In the event of a major fiber
release the procedures set forth below will be implemented to isolate the area. Any response
action that is necessary to remedy a major fiber release episode must be designed by an
ACCREDITED PROJECT DESIGNER and conducted by an ACCREDITED
CONTRACTOR/SUPERVISOR AND WORKERS.

12, PERIODIC SURVEILLANCE

Periodic surveillance will be carried out at least every six months by personnel familiar
with the Nye County School District's inspection and assessment procedures, All known and
assumed asbestos-containing material (ACM) will be visually inspected and assessed. Each
assessment will be documented using the Periodic Surveillance Assessment Form (see following
page). Each assessment will be signed and dated by the assessor. Assessments will be submitted
to the Inspector/Trainer/Supervisor for review and inclusion in the appropriate Management
Plan.

Should the assessment reveal a change in condition of ACM, the Nye County School
District's Asbestos Program Coordinator will be notified in writing. The Coordinator will
determine whether the Nye County School District should initiate a response action in view of
the change. ;

===== PAGE 287 =====
13. DISPOSAL OF ASBESTOS WASTE
_ The Nye County School District will follow accepted methods and regulatory
requirements regarding the disposal of asbestos-containing waste resulting from O&M activities,
All asbestos-containing debris or waste will be bagged at the originating site in labeled 6-
mil polyethylene bags. These bags are chosen because they are air-tight and puncture resistant.
Bags will be labeled with the OSHA required statement:
DANGER
CONTAINS ASBESTOS FIBERS
AVOID CREATING DUST
CANCER AND LUNG DISEASE HAZARD
In addition to the OSHA labeling requirement, the Department of Transportation (DOT) requires
additional labeling on any bag or container with more than one pound of asbestos waste. Proper
DOT wording is as follows:
RQ HAZARDOUS
| SUBSTANCE
| SOLID, NOS,
| ORM-E, NA9188
(ASBESTOS)
The meaning of this marking is:
RQ - reportable quantity, i.c., more than a pound.
Nos "not otherwise specified", refers to the classification on DOT's hazardous
materials table.
ORM-E - designation for the hazard class for asbestos.
NA9188 - identification code of the United Nations marking system for hazardous
/ materials.
To avoid confusion as to whether the amount of waste in any one bag meets or exceeds
the DOT reportable quantity, the OSHA and DOT markings will appear on all 6-mil polyethylene
| disposal bags.
|
| Bagged asbestos-containing waste will be transported by trained O&M staff to the Nye
County School District's temporary waste storage facility upon completion of O&M activities.
Bags will be placed in plastic lined aluminum containers with lids. Labels on the outside of each
container will be identical to those on the bags. The container will be covered after waste bags
have been deposited within and kept in a secured area. Each container will be used to its
113


===== PAGE 288 =====
. maximum capacity, Once it is full the labeled liner will be closed around the contents, twisted

» and folded over, and taped shut. The list of sites from which the waste was generated will be
fixed to the bag. Waste bags shall be maintained in a designated temporary holding area. Once
this area reaches holding capacity, the O&M Program Coordinator will be notified. A trained
O&M worker will be assigned to transport asbestos waste to the landfill.

Asbestos-containing waste will be transported to designated and approved landfills.
During transport, labeled bags of waste will be packed to prevent shifting or splitting. They must
be covered during transport. All requisite documentation will accompany asbestos-containing
waste to the disposal site.

Once the truck arrives at the landfill, ACM disposal bags will be taken out of the vehicle
along with other polyethylene wrapped ACM. All waste will be placed in the specified area at
the landfill site. To avoid breaking ACM disposal bags, they will be placed on the ground, not
dropped off the truck. While unloading the truck, workers will wear their assigned respirator.

Documentation of disposal will be forwarded to the O&M Program Coordinator and
placed in the Nye County School District's permanent files.

; 14. PERSONAL AIR MONITORING REQUIREMENTS

The OSHA Asbestos Exposure Rule for Construction Workers (29 CFR 1926.1101) sets
forth specific requirements for air monitoring in workplace settings. These requirements apply to
O&M programs in buildings with ACM; the air monitoring provisions in the Nye County School
District's O&M Program outlined below are designed to satisfy the OSHA requirements.

14.1 OSHA REQUIREMENTS

OSHA requires that initial monitoring be conducted at the initiation of any activity that
may result in exposure to asbestos. Although a specific number of days of monitoring is not
specified, the data collected must be statistically reliable. In addition, a representative worker for
each task performed must be monitored, and new monitoring must be conducted at the initiation
of each new activity unless the type of asbestos material and the work practices are exactly the
same as previously monitored activities.

OSHA compliance monitoring is the responsibility of any employer whose employees are
or may be, subject to asbestos exposure. Under this O&M Program the Nye County School
District must develop and conduct an air monitoring program to satisfy OSHA requirements.
The Nye County School District will document and maintain records of personal air monitoring
for each O&M activity for each type of ACM described in the Nye County School District's
O&M Program.

If the initial monitoring conducted by the Nye County School District shows all levels
below the OSHA standards, subsequent monitoring would not be needed. However, if some
observed levels are above the standards, additional monitoring would be needed on an
intermittent basis. In this event, the exact frequency should be determined in consultation with
the Nye County School District's asbestos consultant and OSHA enforcement officials.

14.2 AIR MONITORING PROCEDURES

All air monitoring will be conducted in strict conformance with OSHA regulations. As
an aid to the O&M Program Coordinator when issuing an air monitor to Asbestos Maintenance
Team Members, see the Personal Air Monitoring Checklist at the end of this section.

Equipment

Personal air samplers are used for this type of air monitoring. These are portable, battery-
operated pumps, with associated tubing and 25-mm filter cassettes with 0.8mm micro cellulose
ester filters. The O&M Program Coordinator will ensure that all equipment is maintained in good

operating condition.
Procedures
Two types of samples must be collected; 30 minute monitoring
for OSHA Excursion Limit (EL) compliance and full shift monitoring for Permissible Exposure
Limit (PEL) compliance. If several different tasks at different sites are performed, by an
; employee, several sets of samples must be collected. For example, if an employee performed
three asbestos-related O&M tasks during one day, the sampling would be as follows:
116


===== PAGE 291 =====
Task Duration of Work Sampling Times Type
1 2.0 hours 2.0 hours PEL ‘
0.5 hours EL
2 1.5 hours 1.5 hours PEL
0.5 hours BL
3 3.0 hours 3.0 hours PEL ,
0.5 hours EL
The 30-minute samples will indicate short term exposure levels for OSHA EL
compliance. The full task duration sampling will indicate the average exposure for the full work
shift for OSHA PEL compliance. EL and PEL sampling will be conducted simultaneously,
therefore, if only one employee is assigned to a job, he/she will wear two separate sampling
pumps to collect these samples. If two employees are assigned to a job, one will wear a pump
and sample for the EL, the other for the PEL. The EL samples must be collected during the 30-
minute period of peak exposure to airborne asbestos.
For the tasks shown on the table above, three 30-minute samples would be collected and
three other samples for the remaining times. Altogether, sampling would cover the entire 6.5
hour work period.
Filter Analysis
Each filter cassette must be sent to a qualified lab for analysis by the OSHA Reference
Method. Consult 29 CFR 1926.1101 for details on sample handling, transport, quality control,
and analytical techniques.

15. OUTSIDE CONTRACTOR NOTIFICATION

All outside service contractors who in the course of performing their services may disturb
ACM will be notified and required to sign a written statement acknowledging that they have been
informed of the presence, type, and location of ACM in Nye County School District Buildings.
In addition, they will receive notice of the Nye County School District's O&M Program and the
conditions they must honor regarding this Program.

Utility companies and plumbing, electrical, mechanical, roofing, cleaning, and other
service contractors are included in this provision of the O&M Program. A copy of the written
statement to be signed by outside contractors is shown as Exhibit 15-1.

If the Nye County School District chooses to negotiate contracts with on-call asbestos
abatement companies, these contracts will inchide the following elements:

e Copies of the Nye County School District's respiratory protection program,

medical surveillance program, and AHERA training documentation.

° A resume for each Nye County School District's crew chief (the "competent

person" in the language of the OSHA asbestos rule).

These elements do not have to be evaluated. Their inclusion in contracts with Nye
County School District will be evidence that the contractor is (1) familiar with OSHA
requirements for controlling asbestos exposure, (2) has experience working around ACM, and (3)
has the necessary accreditation to perform work in Nye County School District buildings.

120


===== PAGE 295 =====

OUTSIDE CONTRACTOR STATEMENT
I, representing
(name) (company)
have been informed by the Operations and Maintenance (O&M) Program Coordinator of the Nye
County School District of the presence of asbestos-containing materials (ACM) in the building(s)
for which we provide contract services. I have reviewed the attached documentation showing the
type and location of ACM found in the affected building(s) on the(se) site(s).
J have been informed that to ensure safe conditions in the buildings which our company
services the Nye County School District has instituted an O&M Program. The purpose of the
O&M Program is to minimize the release and distribution of asbestos fibers by controlling
activities that might disturb the ACM. Our company and its employees will assist by observing
the following conditions:
| ° Preventing inadvertent or intentional ACM disturbance,
| e Reporting any accidental disturbance of ACM immediately to the Nye County
; School District's O&M Program Coordinator (named below),
e Reporting any observed change in the condition of ACM to the O&M Program
Coordinator, and
° Where disturbance of ACM is pussible ur canuoi be avoided, cmploying work
practices as specified in the O&M Program, (applicable sections are attached).

|
|

I am aware of the potential health risks associated with exposure to asbestos fibers. The
company I represent has informed our employees regarding the potential hazards and the location
of ACM in the buildings which they service. Our employees are aware of their obligation to
comply with the above conditions.
As a representative of I am authorized to provide the above assurances to the
Nye County School District and obligate the company to the above conditions. In addition, I
provide assurance that our company is aware of and in compliance with all applicable Federal
and State regulations governing ACM and worker safety.
Authorized Company Representative

lc Environmental Inspection & Control Services

CLIENT
Nye County School District
Mr. Robert Whimpey
601 Calvada Blvd.
Pahrump, NV 89048
PROJECT LOCATION
Tonopah Middle School Library
Tonopah, NV
PURPOSE OF INSPECTION
Limited mold investigation
REFERENCE
Exhibit A
Exhibit B
Natural Link Mold Lab Control ID 21764
Background
EICS was engaged by the client to inspect and assess the library at the Tonopah Middle School
for evidence of fungal contamination. District employees had complained of persistent odor within
the library. The inspection was performed on Oct 13, 2009 by Lawrence G. Thir, owner and
senior hygienist of EICS. Access was provided by a representative of NCSD.
Inspection
| visually inspected all areas within the library and adjoining rooms for visible fungal growth. Then,
using a Thomas high volume air sampling pump with a 37 mm Allergenco-D Spore Trap cassette,
| collected three area air samples within the library. One was collected from the HVAC system
ducts, and another was collected outdoors to provide an ambient baseline for reference. All the
pre-numbered samples cassettes were sealed and logged on a chain of custody, then later taken
directly to Natural Link Mold Lab in Reno, NV for analysis by direct non-culturable Fungal
Microscopic Examination (FME).
Findings
The following conditions were observed;
e No visible fungal growth was observed within the library or surrounding rooms.
e Asmall area of water stain was present on a 2x4 lay-in ceiling tile (see exhibit B).
e A distinct chemical odor was present within the library, and to a much lesser extent, the
. adjoining hall.
e The air sample results were unremarkable.
Laboratory findings
Sample no. Location Fungal spore genera Spore/m3
458382 Outdoor, ambient Cladosporium 130
Other 177

Penicillium/Aspergillus 53

Penicillium/Aspergillus 27
Other _65
Total 222
Discussion
The fungal spore genera are consistent with those found outdoors on the baseline sample and in
numbers commonly found in most occupied buildings. All the indoor air sample spore counts
were below those found outdoors.
The odor, mentioned above, seemed to be of an industrial chemical base, probably emanating
from the wall-to-wall carpeting or its’ mastic. The library is constructed with very little outdoor
ventilation from windows or doors and the HVAC system appears to be designed with minimal
outdoor air source. The lack of fresh air from outdoors and continuous recycling of the air in the
HVAC system extend and contain the chemical odor.
Recommendations
As a temporary measure, on days with moderate weather after school hours, the doors
should be opened and fan units used to circulate fresh air through the classrooms for several
hours. This should be done 2-3 times per week for 2 weeks, then once per week until the
odor has dissipated. A permanent correction would involve replacement or alteration of the
HVAC system to provide fresh air source rather than recycling of the indoor air.
The water damaged ceiling tile should be replaced and the source of the water intrusion onto
it corrected.
Limitation
This report provided herein, applies only to the area of the building, discussed. No representation
is made to the condition of any area of the building not addressed. Destructive/invasive
investigation was not performed.
Thank you for the opportunity to be of service. If you have any question, please call me at
(775)786-2800.

PROJECT LOCATION
Tonopah Elementary School
4220 Idaho Circle
Tonopah, NV
PURPOSE OF INSPECTION
Air clearance monitoring following removal of asbestos containing material
REFERENCE
| AT L Laboratories Reports
Exhibit A
Background

Between the dates of August 11, 2008 and August 13, 2008 EICS was engaged by the client to
perform baseline inspections, area air monitoring, and clearance air monitoring in the gymnasium
concession room, elementary school classrooms 30 and 34. The age of the building is
approximately 50 years. Advance Installations, Inc., a State licensed asbestos abatement
contractor removed approximately 2800 square feet of carpet and asbestos containing vinyl
acoustic floor tile and mastic from the above noted locations.

: Inspections
8/7/08- Prior to my arrival baseline air samples were performed by Lauren T. Speelman outside of
classrooms 30 and 32. Mrs. Speelman set and calibrated two Thomas high volume air sampling
pumps with 25 mm cassettes within the vicinity of the abatement project scheduled for 8/11/08
thru 8/13/08. After drawing approximately 1230 liters of air, the pre-numbered cassettes were
sealed and logged onto a chain-of-custody for analysis by Phase Contrast Microscopy (PCM),
using NIOSH method 7400.
8/11/08- Upon my arrival | found a full negative pressure containment with three-stage
decontamination unit and HEPA filtration at classrooms 30 and 34. | inspected the outside of the
containment around the critical barriers around the classrooms to ensure that they were properly
sealed. | performed area air monitoring outside of classroom 30 at the negative air exhaust point.
| set and calibrated one Thomas high volume air sampling pumps with a 25 mm cassette. After
drawing approximately 1764 liters of air, the pre-numbered cassette was sealed and logged onto
a chain-of custody for analysis by Phase Contrast Microscopy (PCM), using NIOSH method
7400.
8/12/08- | performed area air monitoring during abatement at the following locations: outside of
classroom 34 at the negative air exhaust point, at the decontamination unit in classroom 36, and
at the negative air exhaust point in classroom 36. | set and calibrated a total of four high volume
air sampling pumps with 25 mm cassettes. After drawing approximately 1836 liters of air, the pre-
numbered cassettes were collected and logged onto a chain-of-custody for PCM analysis. | also
performed baseline air monitoring at the south end of classroom 36. | set and calibrated one high
volume air sampling pump with a 25 mm cassette. After drawing 1250 liters of air the pre
numbered cassette was collected and logged onto a chain-of-custody for PCM analysis.
| proceeded to the gymnasium concession room and performed baseline air monitoring at the
concession room door. | set and calibrated one Thomas high volume air sampling pump with a
25 mm cassette. After drawing 1352 liters of air, the pre-numbered cassettes here sealed and
logged onto a chain-of-custody for PCM analysis.

2900 Vassar Street #503 PH. (775) 786-2800

Reno, NV 89502 FAX (775) 786-9599


===== PAGE 366 =====
Upon completion of the abatement and detail cleaning at classroom #30, | performed a visual
inspection of the containment. | found the work area to be clean and free of dust, debris, and
residue. The contractors sprayed the containment with a light coat of clear lock down
encapsulant. Once the area was dry | set and calibrated five high volume air sampling pumps
with TEM cassettes. After drawing approximately 2192 liters of air, the pre-numbered cassettes
were sealed and logged onto a chain-of-custody for laboratory analysis by Transmission Electron
Microscopy, using AHERA 40 CFR 763.

Following the final inspection and clearance air monitoring at classroom #30, the abatement
project in classroom #34 was complete. | performed a visual inspection of the second
containment and found the work area to also be clean and free of any and all residual acm debris.
| set and calibrated five Thomas high volume air sampling pumps with TEM cassettes. After
drawing approximately 1417 liters of air, the pre-numbered cassettes were sealed and logged
onto a chain-of-custody for TEM analysis.

8/13/08- | returned to the gymnasium concession room to perform area air monitoring during
abatement. | set and calibrated on high volume air sampling pump with a 25 mm cassette. After
drawing 1267 liters of air the pre-numbered cassette was sealed and logged onto a chain-of-
custody. Upon completion of abatement | performed a final visual inspection and air clearance
monitoring in the gymnasium concession room. Following removal and detail cleaning the work
area was sprayed with a light coat of clear lock down encapsulant. | set and calibrated five
Thomas air sampling pumps with TEM cassettes. After drawing approximately 1375 liters of air,
the pre-numbered cassettes were sealed and logged onto a chain-of-custody for TEM analysis.

===== PAGE 367 =====
Discussion

/ During abatement the State licensed asbestos. abatement contractor, Advance Installations

: maintained safe conditions in and around the work areas. The critical barriers were properly
sealed, thus preventing contamination in the areas outside of containment. The baseline
samples were well within industry standard. The laboratory results for the clearance air
monitoring indicated the airborne fiber counts within the work areas were well below the 70s/mm?
clearance level established by AHERA. The work areas may be considered safe to reoccupy.
Thank you for the opportunity to be of service. If you have any questions, please call me at (775)
786-2800, or (775) 742-2794.

PROJECT LOCATION
Tonopah Elementary School
Tonopah Middle School

PURPOSE OF INSPECTION
Air clearance monitoring following removal of asbestos containing material
REFERENCE
| AT L Laboratories Reports 8/8/08-8/9/08
Exhibit A
Background
Between the dates of August 5, 2008 and August 7, 2008 EICS was engaged by the client to
perform baseline inspections, area air monitoring, and clearance air monitoring in the middle
school hallway, and classroom #28 in the elementary school. The age of the building is
approximately 50 years. Advance Installations, Inc., a State licensed asbestos abatement
contractor removed approximately 1400 square feet of asbestos containing vinyl acoustic floor tile
and mastic from the above noted locations.
Inspections
8/5/08- Upon my arrival | found a full negative pressure containment with three-stage
decontamination unit and HEPA filtration. | inspected the outside of the containment around the
critical barriers at the middle school to ensure that they were properly sealed. | performed area
air monitoring at the North end of the building just outside of the containment. | set and calibrated
two Thomas high volume air sampling pumps with 25 mm cassettes. After drawing approximately
1233 liters of air, the pre-numbered cassettes were sealed and logged onto a chain-of custody for
analysis by Phase Contrast Microscopy (PCM), using NIOSH method 7400.
8/6/08- | returned to the middle school to inspect the three-stage decontamination unit and the
manometer readings to ensure the contractors were maintaining the proper amount of negative
pressure within the containment. | performed area air monitoring adjacent to the decontamination
unit. | set and calibrated two high volume air sampling pumps with 25 mm cassettes. The
process was repeated at the critical barrier in the Northeast classroom, and at the North end of
the building adjacent to the library. After drawing approximately 2489 liters of air, the pre-
numbered cassettes were collected and logged onto a chain-of-custody for PCM analysis.
Upon completion of the abatement and detail cleaning | performed a visual inspection of the
containment. | found the work area to be clean and free of dust, debris, and residue. The ‘
contractors sprayed the containment with a light coat of clear lock down encapsulant. Once the
area was dry | set and calibrated five high volume air sampling pumps with TEM cassettes. After
drawing approximately 1280 liters of air, the pre-numbered cassettes were sealed and logged
onto a chain-of-custody for laboratory analysis by Transmission Electron Microscopy, using
AHERA 40 CFR 763.
During the clearance air monitoring | proceeded to the work area in classroom #28 at the
elementary school. | inspected the work area while the contractors were erecting the
containment. | performed baseline air sampling at this time. | set and calibrated four high volume
! air sampling pumps with 25 mm cassettes. After drawing approximately 1947 liters of air the
: cassettes were collected and logged onto a chain-of-custody for PCM analysis.
2900 Vassar Street #503 PH. (775) 786-2800
Reno, NV 89502 FAX (775) 786-9599


===== PAGE 369 =====
8/7/08- | performed an inspection of all critical barriers, and a final visual inspection and air
oo clearance monitoring in classroom #28. | found a full negative pressure containment with three
stage decontamination unit. Following removal and detail cleaning the work area was sprayed
with a light coat of clear lock down encapsulant. | set and calibrated five Thomas air sampling
pumps with TEM cassettes. After drawing approximately 1213 liters of air, the pre-numbered
cassettes were sealed and logged onto a chain-of-custody for TEM analysis.

Discussion
During abatement the State licensed asbestos abatement contractor, Advance Installations
maintained safe conditions in and around the work areas. The critical barriers were properly
sealed, thus preventing contamination in the areas outside of containment. The baseline
samples were well within industry standard. The laboratory results for the clearance air
monitoring indicated the airborne fiber counts within the work areas were well below the 70s/mm?
clearance level established by AHERA. The work areas may be considered safe to reoccupy.

786-2800, or (775) 741-4748.
